**Why.** In a pool this small, the constraint is knowing who is free, not who exists. Whoever holds the register controls the schedule.

**Here.** With roughly 1,000 film FTEs nationally, two concurrent large shows can absorb the entire available pool; the register is how you see the collision six weeks out instead of on the Monday.

#### Trade apprenticeships converted from Icelandic construction trades

ESTIMATE: 24 apprentice places a year across scenic carpentry, scenic art, metalwork, rigging and electrics, run inside Iceland's iðnnám/sveinspróf apprenticeship system with a named journeyman mentor per apprentice and a 3-year indenture.

**Why.** Construction, rigging and paint are the largest below-the-line headcounts and the easiest to grow domestically — they are trades before they are film jobs.

**Here.** Iceland has a functioning statutory apprenticeship and journeyman-exam system for building trades; recruit from Tækniskólinn and the building trades rather than from film schools.

#### Published rate card and preferred-vendor list

ESTIMATE: annual rate card covering stage, workshop, office, parking, power at cost-plus, crew grades and every on-lot vendor's headline rates, with a 12-month price hold for bookings over 12 weeks. REAL: Cinespace Chicago publishes preferred vendors incl. Keslow Camera, AbelCine, Cinelease and Periscope Post & Audio.

**Why.** Line producers budget from published numbers. A lot that quotes bespoke every time loses to one that can be budgeted in an afternoon.

**Here.** ISK volatility means a rate card must state its currency and its FX reset; most incoming productions budget in USD, GBP or EUR.

#### Vendor service-level agreements with response times

ESTIMATE: on-lot vendors contract to 30-minute response and 2-hour replacement in stage hours for camera, lighting, grip and expendables; 4-hour for props and costume; penalties tied to lost-hour value at the stage's day rate.

**Why.** The whole premium of an on-lot vendor is the response time. Unwritten, it evaporates the first time the vendor is busy elsewhere.

**Here.** There is no second supplier down the road. Without a contractual SLA, a single Icelandic vendor's bad week becomes your lost shooting day.

#### Insurance broker, completion bond liaison and claims desk

ESTIMATE: resident broker one day a week; lot carries public liability to ISK 2bn+ per event, employer's liability, property, and a stage-specific water-tank/immersion endorsement; productions must evidence cover before load-in and name the lot as additional insured.

**Why.** An uninsured production drowning a camera in your tank becomes your loss. Verification at load-in is the only cheap moment to catch it.

**Here.** Seismic and volcanic-ash exposure changes the property market's appetite; Iceland's Natural Catastrophe Insurance (Náttúruhamfaratrygging Íslands) covers some perils compulsorily — confirm what it excludes for a commercial lot.

#### Animal wrangling and livestock handling

ESTIMATE: contracted wrangler with stabling for 12 horses, quarantine-compliant handling, and an American Humane-style on-set monitor for any animal action.

**Why.** Animal action stops a production dead if welfare paperwork is wrong, and streamers require documented oversight.

**Here.** MAST import rules on live animals are strict and Icelandic horses that leave the country may never return — so animal action is sourced domestically or not at all.

#### Lot-employed standby trades pool

ESTIMATE: 14 permanent staff — 4 carpenters, 3 riggers, 3 electricians, 2 plumbers, 2 painters — chargeable to productions at a published hourly rate and otherwise maintaining the estate.

**Why.** Between bookings a lot still needs trades; during bookings a production needs them at an hour's notice. One pool solves both and stops crew leaving the island between jobs.

**Here.** Permanent employment is the retention answer in a country where the alternative to your gap week is a tourism or construction job that does not release them back.

#### Off-lot vendors that should stay off-lot

ESTIMATE: SFX explosives and pyrotechnics store (site-licensed, separation distance), armoury, large-scale VFX, ADR/mix theatres, and specialist crane/technocrane hire — all called in by the day rather than housed.

**Why.** Some trades bring licensing burden, noise or idle capital that a four-stage lot cannot amortise. Hosting them buys risk, not resilience.

**Here.** Icelandic firearms law is restrictive and an on-lot armoury is a licensing project of its own; pyrotechnic stores need separation distances that compete with buildable land.

### State of the art

#### Vendor lease structure — anchor terms, turnover rent, fit-out contribution

ESTIMATE: 10-year anchor leases for camera and lighting/grip, 5-year for the rest; base rent ~ISK 2,200–3,000/m2/month plus 3–5% turnover rent above a break; lot funds shell + power, tenant funds fit-out; break clause tied to the lot's own occupancy.

**Why.** A pure market rent on a lot with one customer is unlettable; turnover rent shares the risk that the stages go dark and keeps the vendor solvent through a quiet quarter.

**Here.** Rebate law (Act 43/1999) expires 31 Dec 2028; any lease running past that date must price the policy cliff or carry a rebate-lapse break.

#### Hair, make-up and prosthetics lab

ESTIMATE: 400 m2 — silicone and foam-latex run room with extraction and a 60 L vacuum chamber, life-casting bay, wig room with 200 lace fronts, 12-chair make-up room at CRI>96 daylight. REAL: RVK/Truenorth are setting up a make-up studio with Ásta Hafþórsdóttir.

**Why.** Prosthetics is a lead-time trade. Off-island it is a 4-day freight risk on a face that has to match yesterday's continuity.

**Here.** Silicones, adhesives and solvents are dangerous-goods imports on a weather-dependent sailing; hold 12 weeks of consumables, not 4.

#### TPN assessment for the lot and for every on-lot vendor touching content

REAL: TPN assessments are run against the MPA Content Security Best Practices v5.3 framework, with Blue Shield (self-attested) and Gold Shield (third-party assessed) statuses published on TPN+; a Gold cycle typically runs 3–6 months from kickoff to report.

**Why.** Major studios and streamers write TPN status into vendor agreements. An unassessed post room or dailies vendor on your lot is a reason for a show to book elsewhere.

**Here.** Every Icelandic vendor you invite on-lot will need bringing up to the standard; budget the assessment and remediation for them, because none of them will fund it alone.

#### Connectivity — dual-path subsea capacity and a production network

ESTIMATE: 2 x 10 Gbit/s diverse fibre to Reykjavik landing, dual-homed onto separate subsea systems (FARICE-1, DANICE, IRIS), per-production VLANs, 1 Gbit/s guaranteed per stage, Sohonet/Media Network peering.

**Why.** Remote colour sessions, virtual production and live client review are now table stakes. A single fibre path on an island is a single point of failure for the whole lot.

**Here.** Iceland's international capacity is entirely subsea; buy diversity across separate cable systems or accept that one anchor strike takes the campus off the air.

#### The 80% rule as a procurement policy, not an accident

REAL: if more than 80% of the total cost of producing the film or programme is incurred in Iceland, reimbursement is calculated on the total production cost incurred across the European Economic Area (Act 43/1999).

**Why.** Crossing 80% converts European spend into rebated spend. Sourcing decisions made department by department will drift under the threshold without anyone noticing.

**Here.** A lot that hosts camera, lighting, grip, props, costume and post on site is mechanically pushing productions over 80%. That is the commercial argument for the vendor village in one line.

#### Foreign expert tax deduction — administered for imported HODs

REAL: 25% of salary exempt from tax for the first three years; the applicant must not have been resident in Iceland in the 60 months before starting, the expertise must be unavailable or scarce in Iceland, and the application goes to Rannís within 3 months of starting work.

**Why.** On a three-year build-and-run, this is roughly a 25%-of-salary swing on every imported head of department — the difference between affording a first-choice HOD and not.

**Here.** UNCERTAIN whether short-engagement film HODs are assessed as qualifying experts; the scheme is written around R&D, teaching, project and construction management. Get a ruling before promising it in an offer.

#### On-lot crew lodge

ESTIMATE: 60–90 keys, en-suite, blackout blinds mandatory, 24-hour kitchen, laundry, drying room, quiet-hours policy; walking distance to the stages so a 06:00 call needs no drive in the dark.

**Why.** Removes the worst hour of a winter day — the commute — and lets the lot absorb a schedule change without renegotiating hotels.

**Here.** June has effectively no darkness and December almost none: blackout blinds in summer and full-spectrum lighting in winter are functional requirements for people who must sleep on a rota.

#### Blackout and daylight design in every crew space

ESTIMATE: blackout to <1 lux in all sleeping rooms; circadian-tuned lighting in production offices and green rooms at 500 lux/6,500 K mornings falling to 300 lux/3,000 K evenings; vitamin D provision Oct–Mar.

**Why.** Two opposite failures a year — no sleep in June, no daylight in December — both show up as errors, sickness and turnover.

**Here.** At 64°N, midsummer twilight never ends and midwinter gives roughly four hours of usable light. Both are scheduling and health facts, not weather.

#### Gym, pool, sauna and physiotherapy

ESTIMATE: 300 m2 gym, geothermally heated 25 m pool and hot pots, two saunas, two physio rooms with a resident sports physio 3 days/week.

**Why.** Grip, construction and rigging are heavy trades with chronic injuries; on-site physio turns a two-week absence into a two-hour appointment.

**Here.** Geothermal district heat makes a heated pool and hot pots nearly free to run, and pool culture is the Icelandic social default — this is the amenity that makes local crew choose your lot.

#### Childcare on the lot

ESTIMATE: 40-place leikskóli-standard nursery open 06:30–19:00, matching Icelandic municipal ratios, plus a school-holiday club. Cost recovered at municipal rates with the lot subsidising the extended hours.

**Why.** Film hours are incompatible with standard nursery hours. Childcare is the retention lever for the exact mid-career crew a small pool cannot replace.

**Here.** Iceland has the highest female labour participation in the OECD and near-universal preschool use; a lot that ignores childcare simply loses those crew to broadcast and tourism employers.

#### Trainee places bonded to every stage booking

ESTIMATE: contract term requiring 1 paid trainee per 25 crew (typically 6–12 per show), paid at the lot's expense not the production's, with a named HOD mentor, minimum 8 weeks, and a logbook signed off against the grade.

**Why.** Voluntary trainee schemes collapse the first time a show runs late. Written into the booking, the pipeline survives the schedule.

**Here.** This is how you get from 1,000 to 1,500 crew in five years. Truenorth's Kristinn Thordarson has publicly flagged the shortage of local skilled crew and the need for training initiatives.

#### HOD step-up programme — shadowing the imports you pay for

ESTIMATE: 8 shadow-HOD placements a year (gaffer, key grip, production designer, 1st AD, construction manager, VFX supervisor), each a full-schedule paid attachment to the imported HOD, with the import contractually required to take the shadow.

**Why.** Imported HODs are the most expensive and least replaceable input. If they leave without a successor, you buy them again next year at a higher price.

**Here.** HODs are imported and housed by default here; making the shadow a term of engagement is the only mechanism that converts that spend into national capacity.

#### Concierge and settling-in service for imported crew

ESTIMATE: 2 FTE handling kennitala registration, bank accounts, housing viewings, school places, driving licence conversion, winter-driving induction and a partner-employment introduction. REAL: Trilith PLUS explicitly includes sourcing and leasing accommodation for cast, crew and VIPs.

**Why.** HODs turn down jobs over logistics, not money. The partner who cannot get a job and the child who cannot get a school place are what end a three-year posting early.

**Here.** Nothing in Iceland happens without a kennitala. A crew member without one cannot be paid, banked, or registered — and therefore cannot count toward the rebate's crew test.

#### Vendor viability underwriting — the honest cost of a village

ESTIMATE: minimum revenue guarantees of ISK 40–80m/year for the two anchor rental tenants for the first three years, plus fit-out capital, written off against future turnover rent. Total ESTIMATE ISK 400–700m of vendor subsidy over five years.

**Why.** A vendor village on a lot with one customer is a subsidy programme wearing a lease. Pricing it honestly at the outset stops it being discovered as a loss in year two.

**Here.** Trilith's 40 vendors sit inside a 700-acre town with continuous work; Mosfellsbær has four stages and weather. The subsidy is the price of the difference.

### What gets got wrong

- The 35% band is a CREWING test, not just a spend test: Act 43/1999 requires at least 50 crew for at least 50 working days AND at least 30 working days in Iceland including 10 shooting days AND ISK 350m local spend. With roughly 1,000 film FTEs nationally, two concurrent 35% productions consume most of the available pool — the incentive can be un-claimable for the second show even though the money is there.
- Wages qualify only if verifiably taxed in Iceland. A flown-in HOD paid through a US or UK loan-out deletes that salary from the rebate base and does not count toward the 50-crew test. Hosting and locally payrolling imported HODs is therefore a rebate decision, not a hospitality one.
- The 80% rule cuts both ways: exceed 80% of total production cost incurred in Iceland and the rebate base extends to total EEA spend; drift below it and you lose the European base entirely. Department-by-department sourcing will cross that line without anyone noticing unless one person owns the ratio.
- Act 43/1999 expires 31 December 2028. Ten-year vendor leases, apprenticeship indentures and housing portfolios all outlive the law that makes the demand. Every long commitment needs a rebate-lapse scenario, and the lot should assume renewal is political, not automatic.
- Icelandic law makes the American shooting day illegal: 11 continuous hours of rest per 24, a working period that may not exceed 13 hours, one weekly day off attached to the daily rest, and a 48-hour average week over four months (Act 46/1980, Arts. 53-54 on compensating rest). Every SLA, catering window and transport rota must be sized to 13 hours, and travel time is inside it.
- There is no comprehensive film collective agreement in Iceland. FK became a union in 2014 and has publicly complained about producers asking freelancers to waive the 11-hour rest, and has argued that rebate eligibility should be conditioned on compliance with hours, wages, accommodation and hygiene law. Building to the law now is cheaper than being the test case.
- Exclusive vendor deals lose bookings. Large productions travel with their own camera and lighting packages; a lot that blocks them is a lot they do not book. Take a landing fee for yard and shell access instead — and in a country this small, an exclusive also hands one firm the national market, which is a reputational problem.
- The two crew numbers in circulation are not the same number. Roughly 1,000 FTE is the current on-set-ish film workforce; Klapptré's 1,806 FTE (2020) and 3,431 work-years are broader film-production and indirect measures. Quoting them interchangeably will oversize a schedule by 80%.
- Sea freight is the real constraint on expendables and consumables. Rotterdam-Reykjavik is about 4 days in good weather and can be blown out entirely; a stock-out is air freight at a multiple or a lost day. Hold 6-12 weeks of cover on sea-freighted lines, not the 2-4 weeks a European lot would.
- TPN Gold takes 3-6 months from kickoff to published report against MPA CSBP v5.3, and virtually every studio and streamer now writes it into vendor agreements. Every on-lot vendor that touches content needs it, none of them will fund it alone, and the clock starts long before the first booking.
- Hosting a caterer exclusively is a mistake; productions bring their own. Own the licensed kitchen as fixed plant and licence it per production instead — you keep the HACCP compliance, the drainage and the grease interception, and the production keeps its chef.
- Reykjavik's rental market is structurally supply-constrained, with average new-contract rent at ISK 268,926 (Feb 2026) and Reykjavik the highest rent per m2 in the country at ISK 4,041. A studio taking 40 units off the market in a 13,000-person municipality becomes a political actor in Mosfellsbær before it becomes a landlord.
- The foreign-expert 25% tax deduction is real (three years, 60 months prior non-residence, application to Rannís within 3 months) but it is UNCERTAIN that short-engagement film HODs qualify — the scheme is framed around research, teaching, project and construction management. Get a written ruling before putting it in an offer letter.
- The ten-working-day posted-worker trigger under Act 45/2007 is low enough that a specialist visiting four times a year crosses it. Foreign SFX, rigging and VFX crews routinely do this without registering; enforcement lands on the production and, by association, the lot.
- Nothing works without a kennitala. Unregistered crew cannot be banked, payrolled or taxed in Iceland, and therefore cannot count toward the rebate's crew test — the concierge function is load-bearing on the incentive, not a perk.
- Volunteer trainee schemes die on the first late schedule. Unless trainee places are written into the booking contract and paid by the lot rather than the production, the pipeline exists only in quiet years — which are exactly the years you do not need it.
- Winter darkness (roughly 4-5 usable hours in December) and midsummer's absent night both hit crew before they hit the schedule: blackout in June, full-spectrum light and vitamin D in December. On a small pool, sickness and turnover are capacity losses you cannot hire around.

---

## 8. Security, confidentiality and privacy at campus scale

*Sugar Land Studios, Mosfellsbær, Iceland*

### Essential

#### Three-ring perimeter geometry

Ring 1: the 809 ha land boundary, stock fence and signage only. Ring 2: a secure core fence, estimate 3.0-3.5 km enclosing 35-45 ha of stages, workshops, basecamp, backlot. Ring 3: building envelopes. Estimate 150 m minimum standoff, public road to nearest stage door.

**Why.** One fence around 809 ha is unaffordable and unmonitorable; without a tight core ring every hiker crossing the land becomes an alarm.

**Here.** Nature Conservation Act No. 60/2013 Art. 18 lets anyone walk uncultivated land without the owner's permission. Owners in settled areas may restrict only on fenced ground — so only the fenced core is lawfully closed.

#### Anti-climb fence engineered for Icelandic wind

358 mesh (76.2 x 12.7 mm aperture, 4 mm wire), 3.0 m plus 0.5 m topping, hot-dip galvanised to ISO 1461 over polyester. Posts 2.4-2.75 m centres in concrete. Design to ÍST EN 1991-1-4; Iceland carries among the highest basic wind velocities in Europe (estimate vb,0 36 m/s here).

**Why.** Palisade and weldmesh sail and fail in a 50 m/s gust, and a downed fence is an open perimeter for the weeks it takes to ship a replacement by sea.

**Here.** Salt spray plus volcanic ash accelerate corrosion. Specify marine-grade coating, stainless fixings, and an annual freshwater washdown.

#### Thermal and radar as primary detection, visible light as secondary

Uncooled LWIR (640x480, 25-75 mm lens) detects a human at 500-1,500 m. Size everything to IEC 62676-4 DORI: detect 25 px/m, observe 62, recognise 125, identify 250 px/m. Identify grade at gates and doors, detect grade on the fence line.

**Why.** Reykjavík has about 4 h 07 min of daylight at the winter solstice; colour cameras and video analytics are effectively blind for twenty hours a day.

**Here.** 21 Dec: sunrise ~11:20, sunset ~15:30. 21 Jun: sunset ~00:03, sunrise ~02:55 and never astronomically dark — so there is also no cover of darkness for moving unannounced assets.

#### Three separated vehicle entries

Main gate 2 in / 1 out with ANPR and 40 m of on-site queuing so trucks never back onto the public road. Freight gate sized for a 16.5 m artic with a 25 m turning circle. Talent gate single-lane, camera-screened, no public sightline to the vehicle.

**Why.** One gate means every car a production owns passes the one spot a photographer can lawfully stand, and a 06:00 freight queue on Vesturlandsvegur.

#### Gatehouse as a building, not a kiosk

Heated, 24/7, 15-20 m2, WC, two officers on shift, direct sightline to both lanes, laminated glass, visitor hold with seating for eight separated from the traffic lane. On the same UPS and generator circuit as the SOC.

**Why.** An unheated kiosk in a Mosfellsbær December means the officer sits in a running car and the gate is unmanned in practice.

#### Vehicle check protocol

Pre-authorised drive-on list closing 16:00 the previous day; ANPR match, driver photo ID, manifest for freight. Load and boot checks by consent only, logged as accept-or-turn-around. 100% check on unlisted vehicles, estimate 10% random on listed.

**Why.** Without a pre-cleared list every arrival is a negotiation at the barrier and the queue backs up exactly at crew call.

**Here.** Icelandic private guards hold no search powers. A search is a condition of entry that can be refused, and refusal means no entry — put that in the licence, not in a guard's discretion.

#### Guard force sizing and licensing

Estimate 5.2 FTE per 24/7 post: gate, SOC and patrol is about 16 FTE plus supervision, before productions buy extra. Security firms are licensed by the police; Securitas hf. (founded 1979, ~400 staff) and Öryggismiðstöðin are the two national providers.

**Why.** An underfunded roster goes dark on night shift, which is precisely when a drone operator or a determined fan walks the fence line.

**Here.** Guards are unarmed, as are most Icelandic police. Their powers are a citizen's: refuse entry, detain only for an offence witnessed, call 112.

#### Access control zoning model

Z0 public road and visitor park; Z1 inside the fence; Z2 production offices and basecamp; Z3 stage and its curtilage, tenant-controlled; Z4 data centre, MER, vault, armoury, SOC; Z5 plant, tank plant room, HV switchroom. Every badge tier maps to a zone set.

**Why.** A single all-areas badge gives catering, cleaning and a visiting investor the same reach as the DIT and the data wrangler.

#### Badging office and identity proofing

Photo badge against passport or national ID, kennitala where held, and a signed NDA. Colour-coded by tier, expiry printed, day passes a different colour and inked with the date. Target issue time under 10 minutes, office open 06:00-20:00.

**Why.** Crew call is 06:00. A badge office opening at 09:00 guarantees the gate waves people through, which is the real failure mode.

**Here.** Imported HODs arrive without a kennitala for weeks. Have a documented non-resident enrolment path or they are escorted guests for a month.

#### Offboarding SLA and access review

Badge and network account disabled within 60 minutes of wrap or termination notice. Every production badge carries a hard expiry at licence end plus three days. Quarterly access recertification; anti-passback and mustering on all Z2 and above readers.

**Why.** Live badges left over from a wrapped season are how last year's grip walks onto this year's unannounced stage.

#### Visitor regime and escort ratios

Visitors signed in against a named host, photographed, NDA at the gate, badge surrendered on exit. Escort 1:5 maximum in Z2, 1:1 in Z3 and Z4. Visitor log retained to a stated schedule and purged, per GDPR minimisation.

**Why.** Unescorted visitors are the leak channel a production cannot see, cannot price and cannot insure against.

#### Three-instrument NDA architecture

A lot-wide mutual NDA signed by every badge holder; a production-specific NDA flowed down by the tenant; a one-page visitor NDA at the gate. Icelandic law, Reykjavík District Court venue, with the tenant free to layer its own US-law instrument above.

**Why.** A US-only NDA against an Icelandic day-player is slow and expensive to enforce; the local instrument is the one that actually bites.

**Here.** Act No. 131/2020 on Trade Secrets implements EU Directive 2016/943 (EEA Joint Committee Decision 91/2019) and gives injunctive relief and damages independent of any contract.

#### Tiered phone and camera policy on stage

T1: lens stickers on personal devices in Z3. T2: phones in numbered lockers at the stage door. T3: Yondr-style locking pouches carried by the individual. Applies identically to studio staff, cleaners, caterers and guards. Bag check at the stage door.

**Why.** The photograph that leaks is almost never taken by camera department; it is a phone in a pocket on the way to lunch.

**Here.** State in the licence which tier the production has bought and whose staff enforce it — the lot's officers or the production's.

#### No lawful jamming, therefore no promises of it

RF jamming and GNSS spoofing are unlawful for private parties under Icelandic electronic communications law. Faraday pouches, lockers and written policy are the only legal camera and phone controls; detection of Wi-Fi and BLE camera signatures is legal but unreliable.

**Why.** Selling a production 'signal suppression' you cannot lawfully deliver is a misrepresentation that becomes a claim the first time an image leaks.

#### Script security chain

Per-recipient watermarked PDFs, visible ID plus invisible forensic mark, via Scenechronize, PIX or equivalent with expiry-based DRM. Sides only to day players. Numbered coloured hard copies signed in and out. Destruction to DIN 66399 P-4 cross-cut or locked secure waste.

**Why.** An unmarked PDF makes a leak untraceable, and traceability to a single recipient is most of the deterrent you are actually buying.

#### Camera media and dailies chain of custody

Media handled only by named DIT and loaders. Two verified copies plus checksum before any card is wiped. Drives in a bolted safe in a Z4 room. Transfers by Aspera, Signiant or Sohonet, or hand-carried by a named person. Forensic watermark on every review link (Moxion, PIX, ClearView Flex).

**Why.** The highest-value object on the lot each night is a 1 TB card in somebody's pocket, and it walks out through the same gate as everyone else.

#### Counter-drone response that is actually legal

Detect and evidence only. On detection: log, track, locate the pilot, dispatch a patrol to their position on public land, photograph, call 112, report to Samgöngustofa. The shoot-downs Marvel is reported to have used on Secret Invasion would be criminal here.

**Why.** Any active mitigation by a private operator is both a radio offence and an aviation offence; the incident record is your only remedy.

**Here.** Ceiling is 120 m AGL and the lot sits outside the 2 km Reykjavík (BIRK) exclusion zone, so a hobby drone overhead is presumptively lawful. The May 2026 Náttúruverndarstofnun permit restrictions cover protected areas, not private land like this.

#### Sightline survey from every public vantage

Photograph the lot from every publicly accessible high point at 600 mm equivalent and log what is legible. Local vantages: Úlfarsfell 291 m, Helgafell 217 m, Mosfell, and Esja/Þverfellshorn at 914 m to the north — all popular, all lawful to stand on.

**Why.** A backlot set laid out without a sightline survey is photographed from a marked hiking trail on its first day of dressing.

**Here.** Right to roam means you cannot close the hills. Set orientation, screening and scheduling are the only controls you own.

#### CCTV coverage design

Estimate 180-260 cameras. Gates and doors at identify grade, 250 px/m. Fence line at detect grade, 25 px/m. Plus stage doors, corridors, MERs, vault, tank plant room, fuel store and waste yard. No cameras inside the stage volumes.

**Why.** Cameras inside a stage make the lot a processor of the tenant's content; missing cameras at the waste yard lose the leak you most need to trace.

#### CCTV retention and Icelandic surveillance rules

Persónuvernd Rules No. 50/2023 on electronic surveillance replaced Rules No. 837/2006. Delete when no longer necessary, with a hard ceiling of 90 days under Art. 11 and narrow exceptions. Signage before every monitored area (Arts. 8-9). Covert surveillance prohibited (Art. 7).

**Why.** Over-retention is the easiest complaint for a disgruntled crew member to raise and the easiest for the regulator to substantiate.

**Here.** Signs must name the controller and the purpose, in Icelandic and English. A DPIA is required for the workplace element under Art. 12, and Art. 10 limits who may be shown footage.

#### Per-tenant network segregation

A VRF or VLAN per tenancy with its own firewall context and no lateral path between productions. 10 GbE to every stage and production office. 1-10 Gbps symmetric internet on two diverse paths, dark fibre to Reykjavík.

**Why.** Two productions on one flat network means one tenant's breach is the other tenant's leak, and the lot is liable to both.

**Here.** International capacity rides FARICE-1, DANICE and IRIS. Diversity means two different cable systems, not two ports on the same one.

#### Incident classification and escalation matrix

Five levels. L1 nuisance: log. L2 trespass or drone: patrol and report. L3 confirmed imagery leak: notify the tenant within 1 hour. L4 personal data breach: Persónuvernd within 72 hours under GDPR Art. 33. L5 life safety or major: 112 and incident command.

**Why.** Without agreed levels a drone sighting and a stolen drive produce the same 3 a.m. phone call and neither gets the right response.

**Here.** CERT-IS, the national CSIRT, is the cyber escalation; the capital-area police (LRH) and 112 are the physical one. Iceland's NIS2 transposition will amend Act No. 78/2019.

#### Continuity of security through ash, quake and outage

SOC, gates, access control and the VMS core on the essential-load bus: 30 minutes UPS plus generator with 72 hours of fuel. Ash protocol: seal SOC and MER intakes, fit gate camera covers, drill manual gate operation. Seismic restraint of racks to ÍST EN 1998.

**Why.** An ashfall or a quake is exactly when perimeter control matters most and exactly when power, network and cameras degrade together.

**Here.** With Landsvirkjun refusing new industrial load, on-site generation is not optional — and the security load must sit inside the guaranteed slice, not compete with the stage.

#### Union consultation and the lawful basis for monitoring

Consult recognised unions before deploying any new monitoring. Document a DPIA under Rules 50/2023 Art. 12. Employee consent is not a valid basis for workplace monitoring given the power imbalance. Publish who may view footage and how to make a subject access request.

**Why.** Monitoring deployed without consultation gets switched off by the regulator mid-shoot, which is far more disruptive than consulting first.

**Here.** With roughly a thousand crew nationally, a fight with the filmmakers' union is a fight with the entire available workforce for years.

#### Security written into the licence, line by line

Schedule it: guard hours included versus charged, zones granted, badge counts, CCTV retention and who may view, phone policy tier, drone response, escort ratios, breach notification (recommend 1 hour for imagery, 24 hours otherwise), audit right, and a GDPR Art. 28 data processing agreement.

**Why.** Every dispute after a leak is about what was promised; an unwritten security service is a claim against the lot rather than a service to the tenant.

#### Confidentiality of the tenancy itself

The lot does not confirm, deny or imply who is shooting. No tenant names in marketing, case studies, LinkedIn posts, planning submissions or on the website until released in writing by the tenant. Tours suspended while an unannounced title occupies Z3.

**Why.** A studio that publicises its bookings will never be trusted with an unannounced title, and that trust is the whole business.

**Here.** Rebate filings under Act No. 43/1999, customs entries and planning documents create a public paper trail. Work with Film in Iceland on what can lawfully be withheld.

### Expected

#### Perimeter intrusion detection that survives gales

Fence-mounted fibre or MEMS accelerometer PIDS zoned at 50-100 m, with thresholds driven live by an on-site anemometer. Backed by ground surveillance radar (Magos, Robin) giving 300-700 m human detection, so the fence sensor can be desensitised in storms without going blind.

**Why.** Wind false alarms above roughly 20 m/s make fence sensors unusable; guards learn to ignore them, and the one real breach is ignored with them.

**Here.** Mosfellsbær sees gale-force wind routinely through winter. A system that cries wolf every storm night is worse than no system at all.

#### Lighting that does not advertise the lot

Full cut-off luminaires, 3000 K or warmer, zero uplight. 20-50 lux at gates and doors, 5-10 lux on patrol roads, fence line lit only on camera trigger rather than permanently flooded.

**Why.** Permanent flood lighting marks the lot from every hill, ruins night backlot shooting, and blooms out thermal analytics at the fence.

**Here.** Aurora traffic pulls over on Þingvallavegur and Vesturlandsvegur on clear winter nights. A lit lot draws long lenses and town complaints in equal measure.

#### Security operations centre, with a mirror

Manned 24/7, built to EN 50518 alarm-receiving-centre principles: dual power, redundant comms, hardened construction. VMS, access control, intercom and drone detection on one operator surface. A second mirrored position in a different building.

**Why.** Alarms nobody is watching are a logbook. A single SOC lost to fire, flood or quake loses the entire system in one event.

**Here.** Seismically active: anchor racks and screens to ÍST EN 1998 and keep a paper gate-list fallback that works with no power at all.

#### Credential technology

MIFARE DESFire EV3 or HID Seos with AES-128 mutual authentication and diversified keys. Never 125 kHz prox, never card serial number alone. Reader to controller on OSDP v2.2 Secure Channel, not Wiegand. Mobile NFC/BLE credential as the crew default.

**Why.** A 125 kHz badge is cloned in two seconds through a jacket by a EUR 20 device, and Wiegand is read off the reader's own wires.

#### Background screening within Icelandic law

Criminal record certificates (sakavottorð) cannot be demanded generally and GDPR Art. 10 restricts conviction data. Screen by role: identity and right to work for everyone; references and financial checks only for cash, vault and IT-admin roles, with documented necessity.

**Why.** Blanket criminal checks are unlawful here and collapse under a Persónuvernd complaint; a role-based policy survives one.

**Here.** With a crew base of roughly a thousand nationally, reputational reference checks through known HODs are more informative than any certificate.

#### Turnstiles and anti-tailgating

Optical speed gates (Fastlane, Boon Edam) in the production office lobby, 500-900 mm aisles with one DDA lane and tailgate detection. Stage personnel doors on card plus PIN or mobile, with door-forced and held-open alarms at 30 seconds.

**Why.** Tailgating is the commonest way an unbadged person reaches a stage, and a door held open in a gale is a wind-load failure as well.

#### Liquidated damages set at a level a court will keep

Fixed-sum penalties (févíti) are enforceable in Iceland but reducible by a court under Art. 36 of the Contracts Act No. 7/1936 if unreasonable. Set a defensible figure — estimate ISK 1-5 m for a crew NDA — rather than a US-style seven-figure number.

**Why.** An unconscionable penalty is struck out, leaving you to prove actual loss, which for an unannounced title is close to impossible.

#### Minors, guardians and chaperones

Under-18s sign through a guardian who is bound in their own right. Chaperone access is a defined badge tier with the same phone policy, not an exception to it. Icelandic working-hours rules for minors govern their presence on the lot, not only their shooting.

**Why.** An unbound parent standing at video village with a phone is the single most common source of on-set photographs.

#### Codename regime with full flow-through

Every tenancy gets an alias used on signage, call sheets, gate lists, catering orders, waste manifests, invoices and the lot's own booking system. Precedent: 'Blue Harvest' for Return of the Jedi, 'Corona' for The Force Awakens.

**Why.** A vendor invoice or a labelled skip tells the town what is shooting weeks before the production intends to announce it.

#### Secure print, scan and confidential waste

Follow-me printing released by badge; scan-to-personal-email blocked. Locked secure-waste consoles emptied by a vetted contractor issuing certificates of destruction. Separate skips for set dressing and artwork, crushed on site before they leave the fence.

**Why.** Skip-diving is a live tabloid method; a discarded storyboard or a concept board is a front page.

**Here.** Waste contracts run through Sorpa or Íslenska gámafélagið. Negotiate a confidentiality clause and destruction certificates into the waste contract, not just a gate rate.

#### The lot's own drone capability

Register as a UAS operator (ISK 5,863 for five years), pilots holding A1/A3 and A2 certificates, Specific category authorisation for anything beyond. Use for post-storm roof and fence inspection and for pre-shoot sightline surveys.

**Why.** You cannot credibly police drones you have never flown, and inspecting a 30 m stage roof after a gale otherwise needs rope access and a day.

#### Physical screening built for wind, not for California

Earth berms 4-6 m where topography allows — permanent, wind-proof, plantable. Shipping-container walls for temporary work. Scrim and debris netting only in sheltered positions; above roughly 25 m/s netting becomes a sail and then a projectile.

**Why.** Scrim is the default answer on every other lot and it is the wrong answer here; a berm is the Icelandic version of a scrim wall.

**Here.** Berms double as wind shelter for basecamp and unit base — one earthworks spend solving a security problem and a comfort problem.

#### Paparazzi and long-lens management

Identify and where possible lease or buy the sightline parcels. Agree parking restrictions with Mosfellsbær at the obvious laybys. Keep a known-photographer log. Brief unit drivers on counter-surveillance along the Vesturlandsvegur and Þingvallavegur approaches.

**Why.** The photograph is usually taken on the road or at the unit move, not at the fence, and the lot's own vehicle schedule is the tell.

**Here.** Iceland has no anti-paparazzi statute. Public photography is lawful; privacy rests on Constitution Art. 71 and Penal Code Ch. XXV (Arts. 228-229).

#### Talent arrival and movement

Covered arrival at the talent gate. Privacy glass to Icelandic vehicle rules, varied routes, no talent names on any call sheet distributed outside Z2, housing addresses never on a gate list. Close protection coordinated by the lot, supplied by the production.

**Why.** The gate's own list of who arrives when is itself the sensitive document, and it sits with the lowest-paid staff on site.

#### VMS, storage sizing and camera hardening

Genetec, Milestone or Avigilon class VMS. H.265 at 12-15 fps; estimate 30 days at full rate plus 60 at reduced rate, inside the 90-day ceiling — roughly 250-400 TB usable on RAID-6 with an offsite copy. Cameras on a dedicated VLAN with no internet route, signed firmware, no default credentials.

**Why.** Cameras are the most commonly botnetted device on any campus and the shortest path from the car park into a production network.

#### Identity and endpoint security for lot-provided IT

SSO with phishing-resistant MFA (FIDO2 passkeys) across all lot systems, no shared accounts, just-in-time privileged elevation, EDR on every managed endpoint, quarterly recertification, immutable air-gapped backups with a tested restore.

**Why.** The lot's own booking, badging and CCTV systems hold who is shooting what and when — a richer target for a leaker than the footage itself.

#### Wireless hygiene across the campus

Per-tenancy SSIDs on WPA3-Enterprise, fully isolated guest network, rogue-AP and unauthorised-hotspot detection, and a surveyed rule that no production SSID is readable beyond the fence line — measure EIRP at the boundary.

**Why.** A production SSID readable from the public road is both an invitation and a public fingerprint of who is in residence.

#### Leak investigation capability, pre-agreed

Retained ability to trace a forensic watermark, pull access logs and correlate CCTV with gate records inside 24 hours. A written protocol agreed with the tenant on who investigates, who interviews, who speaks publicly, and how evidence is preserved for admissibility.

**Why.** A leak with no investigation repeats; an investigation improvised under press deadline breaks Icelandic employment and data law.

**Here.** Employee interviews engage union agreements. Bring the tenant's Icelandic employment counsel in before the first interview, not after it.

#### Fixated persons and threat assessment

A documented process for stalkers and fixated individuals: log every approach, maintain a subject file on a stated lawful basis, escalate to LRH, offer the tenant a formal threat assessment. Gate staff trained to record rather than confront.

**Why.** Someone who turns up three times is a different problem from someone who turns up once, and only a log tells you which you have.

#### Vault, props, wardrobe and armoury

A Z4 vault for scripts, drives and hero props: 60-minute fire rating, no external wall, dual-authorisation entry, dedicated CCTV and intrusion detection to EN 50131 Grade 3. A separately licensed armoury for blank-firing weapons, with police liaison.

**Why.** Hero props and weapons are the two categories that generate a police call and a press story the moment they walk.

**Here.** Icelandic firearms law is restrictive and police-administered. Agree the armoury arrangement with LRH before the first weapon lands, not on the shoot day.

#### Housing and off-lot exposure

Imported HODs and cast are housed in Mosfellsbær and Reykjavík. Vet the housing provider, bind landlords and housekeepers with the same NDA, avoid clustering the whole cast in one building, and brief arrivals on how fast local information travels.

**Why.** The perimeter stops at the fence; the leak happens at a rented house, a gym, or a bar on Laugavegur.

#### Flow-down to every contract on the lot

The same confidentiality, no-photography and no-social-media terms in every agreement: construction, catering, cleaning, waste, landscaping, coach and taxi operators, fuel delivery, laundry, and the housing provider.

**Why.** The leak comes from the one contract nobody classified as a security contract.

#### Planning for the rebate cliff

The 25% and 35% reimbursement under Act No. 43/1999 runs to 31 December 2028. Expect a crowded 2027-28: more concurrent tenancies, more temporary badges, more contractors, more escorts — then a sharp fall.

**Why.** A badging system and a guard roster sized for two tenants breaks at five, which is precisely when the lot is fullest and most exposed.

### State of the art

#### Hostile vehicle mitigation, proportionate

Speed-reducing chicane holding approach to 25 km/h, plus an IWA 14-1 or PAS 68 rated shallow-mount blocker across the main gate aperture only (class of order V/7200[N3]/48). No rated line elsewhere; standoff does that work.

**Why.** The gate is the only place an unscreened vehicle reaches a crowd of crew; everywhere else distance already solves it.

**Here.** Iceland's terrorism threat is low and over-fortification reads as paranoia to the town and the council. Chicane plus one rated gate is the honest specification.

#### Key management

Electronic-cylinder keys (Abloy Protec2 CLIQ or CyberLock) on all Z3-Z5 doors: audit trail, time windows, remote revoke with no lock change. Mechanical master suite on a patent-protected restricted profile, with a per-key accountable cabinet.

**Why.** A mechanical master lost on a night shoot rekeys a campus; an electronic key is revoked in thirty seconds from the SOC.

#### Multi-sensor drone detection

(a) Remote ID receiver — EU 2019/945 class-marked drones broadcast direct Remote ID; (b) RF detection, DedroneSensor or DroneShield, 1-5 km typical; (c) X-band micro-doppler radar, Robin Elvira class, 1-3 km on a 1 kg quad; (d) PTZ slew-to-cue for evidential identification.

**Why.** A single RF sensor misses an autonomous drone flying a pre-programmed route with the controller switched off — which is exactly the leak case.

**Here.** Iceland implements EU 2019/947 and 2019/945 through Regulation 1360/2024; operators register at flydrone.is. Detection of a Remote ID broadcast is passive and lawful.

#### TPN+ assessment of the lot as a vendor

Assess the facility against the MPA Content Security Best Practices through TPN+, questionnaire v5.3.1. Since September 2025 the tiers are Blue (self-assessed), Silver (remediation plan filed), Gold (remediated and reviewed), Gold Star (plus Additional Recommendations). Blue valid 12 months, Gold 24.

**Why.** Studio tenants increasingly make TPN status a condition of the deal; without it the lot answers a bespoke 200-question vendor form for every production.

#### MovieLabs CSAP alignment

Build to the MovieLabs Common Security Architecture for Production, CSAP v1.3 (2024): zero trust, authorisation at the asset rather than the network, with a Part 3 security level chosen per production. Part 4 covers software-defined workflows.

**Why.** Cloud and remote post mean the network perimeter no longer contains the asset; a lot selling perimeter security alone is selling 2010.

### What gets got wrong

- CCTV retention has a live ambiguity. Persónuvernd's own page on Rules No. 50/2023 states Art. 11 sets a 90-day ceiling with narrow exceptions; several secondary summaries circulate a 30-day figure. Get the Icelandic text confirmed by counsel before sizing storage — the difference is roughly 200 TB.
- You cannot close 809 hectares. Nature Conservation Act No. 60/2013 Art. 18 gives the public passage on foot across uncultivated land without permission; only the fenced core in a settled area may be restricted. Art. 26 further requires gates or stiles where a fence crosses an established path. Budget for a legally open estate and a legally closed core.
- Active counter-drone is criminal here. Jamming, spoofing and kinetic defeat are reserved to state authorities. The Marvel 'we shot one down' anecdote from Secret Invasion is not a template; it is a description of offences. Sell detection and evidence, never mitigation.
- The standard perimeter playbook fails in this wind. Fence-mounted PIDS false-alarms itself into uselessness above roughly 20 m/s, and scrim screening becomes a sail. Radar, thermal and earth berms are the Icelandic substitutes, and they cost more up front.
- Light is inverted twice a year. Twenty hours of darkness in December makes visible-light analytics useless; no darkness at all from mid-May to late July removes the cover of night for moving unannounced assets and gives photographers a 24-hour working day.
- Blanket criminal record checks are unlawful. Sakavottorð cannot be demanded generally and GDPR Art. 10 restricts conviction data. Screen by role with documented necessity, or expect the policy struck down.
- Employee consent is not a lawful basis for workplace monitoring in Iceland — the power imbalance invalidates it. The basis is legitimate interests plus a DPIA plus union consultation, in that order.
- The crew base is about a thousand people nationally. Everyone knows everyone, so the social leak channel is larger than any technical one — and a reputation for heavy-handed security poisons the entire national labour pool, not one production.
- Iceland has no anti-paparazzi statute. Photographing people and property from public land is lawful; Constitution Art. 71 and Penal Code Ch. XXV give only narrow remedies. Screening and geometry are the controls, not law.
- Private guards have no search or detention powers beyond any citizen's, and they are unarmed, as are most Icelandic police. Every vehicle and bag check is a condition of entry that can be refused — so it must be written into the licence, not left to a guard's judgement.
- The grid constraint bites security. With Landsvirkjun refusing new industrial load, security systems must be explicitly placed on the protected generation slice or they will be shed when the stage draws.
- TPN status certifies the facility as a vendor, not the production. Tenants will still impose Netflix, Disney or Amazon requirements on top. Treat TPN as the price of entry, not the finish line.
- Aurora and hiking tourism gives perfect cover for surveillance of the lot. A person with a long lens on Úlfarsfell at midnight, or parked on Þingvallavegur, is doing something entirely normal in Iceland.
- Freight is by sea and weather-dependent. A failed gate blocker, camera housing or fence panel is a four-to-eight-week replacement. Hold spares for every perimeter component with a lead time over 30 days.
- Codenames leak sideways before they leak from people — through invoices, catering orders, waste manifests, courier labels and the lot's own booking software. Flow the alias into finance and facilities, not just onto signage.
- Never put cameras inside a stage volume. The moment you do, the lot becomes a processor of the tenant's content, a target for anyone wanting the images, and a party to any dispute about them.
- Rebate, customs and planning filings create a public paper trail that can identify an unannounced production before the studio announces it. Coordinate with Film in Iceland on what is withheld, and tell the tenant honestly what cannot be.
- The May 2026 Náttúruverndarstofnun drone permit restrictions apply to protected natural areas, not to private land. Do not let anyone on the lot believe drones over Sugar Land are already illegal — under 120 m AGL and outside the BIRK zone, they are presumptively lawful.

---

## 9. Energy, carbon and sustainability at campus scale

*Sugar Land Studios, Mosfellsbær, Iceland (four stages, 13,200 m², 809 ha)*

### Essential

#### Grid connection agreement with reserved firm capacity

Firm 11 kV or 33 kV connection from Veitur's distribution network. Est. 8–12 MVA firm for 13,200 m² of stage plus support at est. 600–900 W/m² connected, 25–35% diversity. The agreement fixes capacity (afl), not consumption.

**Why.** Without contracted capacity you cannot promise a tie-in, and every stage falls back to diesel — the single largest line in any production footprint.

**Here.** Landsvirkjun has publicly stated the system is at full capacity, that it will not prioritise new large-scale industrial customers, and it refused supply to hydrogen/e-fuel developers on those grounds; ESA opened a competition investigation in April 2025. A studio is ~10 MW, not 100+ MW — present it as commercial distribution load through Veitur, not as stóriðja, and sign capacity before ground is broken. Iceland generated 19.06 TWh in 2024, down 3.9% on 2023.

#### Electricity supply contract and price structure

Term contract with Landsvirkjun, ON, HS Orka or Orkusalan. Icelandic business electricity was ISK 12.80/kWh (~USD 0.106) in Dec 2025; large consumers paid ISK 12.55/kWh excl. taxes in Dec 2024 (GlobalPetrolPrices/CEIC). Separate Landsnet/Veitur distribution and capacity charges.

**Why.** Energy is the second-biggest lot overhead after staff; a fixed-term, index-linked contract is what lets you quote all-in stage rates a year out.

**Here.** Cheap by European standards but no longer cheap by Icelandic historical standards — the retail market tightened sharply from 2023 as the system filled. Price is not the risk; availability is.

#### Guarantees of Origin position — declared, deliberate, in writing

Either buy and cancel Icelandic GOs against 100% of consumption, or accept the residual mix. AIB's 2024 European Residual Mixes gives Iceland (IS) 505.51 gCO2/kWh and 0.55 mg/kWh radioactive waste — 71.22% fossil, 18.26% nuclear, only 10.52% renewable, 77.80% untracked.

**Why.** This is the single biggest honesty trap on the lot. Unretired, a production's market-based Scope 2 in Iceland is dirtier than in Britain.

**Here.** Iceland's producers sell renewable GOs into Europe, so the attributes leave the country. AIB shows Iceland drawing 14.05 TWh of attributes FROM the European Attribute Mix in 2024 — roughly its entire consumption. The European average residual mix is ~435 gCO2/kWh, so Iceland's paper grid is worse than average. AIB suspended exports of Icelandic GOs in April 2023 over double-claiming and lifted it that June. Budget est. €2–8/MWh to cancel GOs; it is the cheapest credible line on the whole sustainability budget.

#### Half-hourly metering and sub-metering to the stage, the workshop and the tenant

Revenue-grade meters per stage, per workshop, per office block, per tank plant room, per EV hub. 15-minute or half-hourly data, API-exposed. Separate kWh, kW peak and kVAr. Sub-metered snow melt (Icelandic law requires it for the separate VAT rate).

**Why.** A production cannot report Scope 2 for a stage it shares a meter with. No sub-metering means no albert certification data and no honest recharge.

**Here.** Veitur is rolling out smart meters; insist on remote-read with GSM at every delivery point, which Veitur's snow-melt tariffs already require.

#### Geothermal district heat connection, on the right tariff class

Veitur hitaveita supply. 2025 tariffs: H1 urban ISK 183.33/m³ (207.57 incl. 11% VAT); H4 pools/tanks ISK 91.63/m³ (103.74); V5 general industrial ISK 183.33/m³ (231.88 incl. 24% VAT); V4 process water ISK 91.63/m³ (115.89). Standing charge ≥65 mm meter ISK 255.92/day.

**Why.** Heat is the cheapest input on the lot and the reason a permanently hot water stage is viable here and almost nowhere else.

**Here.** Critical operational fact: tariffs H3, H4 and V1–V5 are explicitly 'ekki forgangsvatn' — non-priority, interruptible at Veitur's discretion, with no discount for curtailment. Your tank heat can legally be cut in a January cold snap. Veitur's peak hot-water demand grows ~120 L/s a year; new wells at Kjalarnes and Geldinganes are 3–5 years out. Negotiate a priority allocation or install an electric backup exchanger.

#### Resilience generation — sized, fuelled and tested

N+1 standby sets, est. 2 × 1,250 kVA, on HVO/renewable diesel (up to ~90% lifecycle CO2 reduction vs fossil diesel) with bunded tanks sized for est. 72 hours at critical load. Monthly on-load testing against a resistive load bank, not off-load idling.

**Why.** Islanded operation without a fuelled backstop is a promise you cannot keep in a week-long February storm. Off-load testing wet-stacks the engines and they fail when needed.

**Here.** Verify HVO supply before specifying it: Iceland's fuel retailers (N1, Olís, Orkan, Atlantsolía) are not confirmed HVO stockists, and imported HVO in drums at Icelandic freight rates is a different economic proposition to a UK bowser. If HVO is not reliably available, say so in the sustainability report rather than implying it.

#### Campus GHG inventory to the GHG Protocol, Scopes 1, 2 and 3

Annual inventory: Scope 1 (gensets, fleet, refrigerants, any combustion), Scope 2 dual-reported (location and market), Scope 3 (construction, freight, staff travel, waste, water, purchased goods, and — declared separately — tenant production emissions). Base year fixed at first full operating year.

**Why.** Everything else — certification, tenders, rebate applications, the owner's own reporting — is downstream of a defensible inventory.

**Here.** Decide early and publicly whether tenant production emissions are inside your boundary. Most studios exclude them and report them as a separate disclosed category; claiming a tiny footprint while four stages burn is the commonest greenwash in the sector.

#### albert production certification support as a stage-hire service

Provide productions with everything the albert carbon calculator asks for: half-hourly stage electricity, heat, water, waste weights by stream, travel data. From 2025 the pass threshold rose from 30% to 55%, productions must answer a wider question set, and unavoidable emissions must be offset to receive the logo.

**Why.** albert is the benchmark required by the BBC, ITV, Channel 4, Sky and UKTV and is increasingly a condition of commission. A lot that cannot supply the data costs its tenant the certification.

**Here.** albert released independently verified Scope 1 and 2 guidance with the Sustainable Entertainment Alliance in August 2025, complementing the 2024 Scope 3 guidance. For an Icelandic lot the Scope 2 question is the GO question — answer it before a production asks.

#### Waste contract, segregation infrastructure and diversion target

Segregation at source across est. 8–12 streams (timber, metal, plastics by polymer, plasterboard, paint, textiles, organics, glass, WEEE, batteries, hazardous, residual). Target ≥90% diversion from landfill and incineration, which is the recognised threshold for effective waste management. Weigh everything.

**Why.** Productions cannot report waste they did not weigh, and a lot that provides only a general skip forces every tenant into an unreportable residual stream.

**Here.** SORPA handles ~200,000 t/yr for the capital region and runs GAJA, the composting and biogas plant at Álfsnes that opened in 2021, as Álfsnes landfill winds down. Iceland's municipal recycling rate was 26% in 2021 against a 55% EEA 2025 target, and 9.3% of the 1.581 Mt processed in 2022 went to landfill. Iceland rejected mass-burn incineration; its one incinerator is for hazardous waste only. So: reuse and recycling are the only real routes, and export is expensive.

#### Catering, food waste and single-use elimination

Reusable crockery and cutlery as the default for est. 200–600 covers a day. Food waste separated to GAJA for biogas and compost. Plumbed-in filtered water stations across the lot and in every stage; no bottled water on site.

**Why.** Catering and single-use are the most visible sustainability signals to a crew; get them wrong and nobody believes anything else the lot claims.

**Here.** Icelandic tap water is among the best in the world and needs no treatment — bottled water on an Icelandic lot is indefensible and reads as absurd to local crew. Organics to GAJA is an existing, functioning municipal route.

#### Cold water supply, metering and leak detection

Metered potable supply from the municipal network with sub-meters on the tank plant room, stages, catering and irrigation. Acoustic or flow-anomaly leak detection on the campus main.

**Why.** Water is the tank stage's defining input; unmetered, you cannot bill it, report it, or notice a leak under 809 hectares of ground.

**Here.** Cold water in Iceland is abundant, unchlorinated groundwater and genuinely cheap — which is exactly why nobody meters it and why leaks run for months. Meter anyway: albert and the Studio Sustainability Standard both ask for water data, and 'it's cheap here' is not an answer.

#### Tank stage water system — volume, turnover, treatment, temperature

2,400 m² tank. At an est. 2 m average working depth that is ~4,800 m³ / 4.8 million litres. Raising it from 4 °C to 30 °C takes ~145 MWh (est., 4,186 kJ/kg·K × 26 K). Filtration turnover target 6 hours. Reference plants: Nu Boyana 20×20×6 m / 2,400 m³, UV filtration, 6-hour turnover, 22 °C heatable to 33 °C, surface skimmers; Pinewood's Underwater Stage held permanently at 30 °C with UV; Kumeu (NZ) 1.267 million litres, 18 m diameter, 4.9 m deep, Hydroxypure ozone/peroxide treatment.

**Why.** Actors spend whole days in this water. Under-filtered or over-chlorinated water closes the stage on health grounds and ruins eyes, skin and costume.

**Here.** The economics are transformed here. Using 80 °C district heat cooled to 30 °C, each m³ of geothermal water delivers ~58 kWh, so a full heat-up needs ~2,500 m³ — at the H4 pool tariff of ISK 103.74/m³ that is ~ISK 259,000 (~EUR 1,750) to fill and heat. Standing loss at an est. 150–400 W/m² is 360–960 kW, ~300 m³/day of geothermal water, ~ISK 31,000/day (~EUR 210). The same heat on UK gas would be est. 15–20× dearer. A permanently hot, permanently available water stage is economically trivial in Iceland and nowhere else. But H4 is non-priority water: contract for a priority allocation or fit an electric backup exchanger.

#### Tank drain-down, discharge consent and effects-water handling

Permitted discharge route for 4,800 m³ with dechlorination/de-ozonation, temperature attenuation and settlement before release. Separate handling for effects water contaminated with fuller's earth, paint, foam, fake blood or fuel residues — tankered as trade effluent, never to surface water.

**Why.** A single uncontrolled drain-down of warm, treated water into an Icelandic watercourse is a prosecutable pollution event and the end of the lot's local licence to operate.

**Here.** Iceland's wastewater infrastructure will not help you: the capital area's plants, the largest being Veitur's Klettagarðar, give mechanical/preliminary treatment only before discharging to the open ocean, and secondary treatment is minimal nationally. A studio discharging anything non-trivial must treat it itself — assume on-site treatment plant, not a connection.

#### Claims register and anti-greenwashing governance

Every public environmental claim logged with its evidence, its boundary, its date and its owner. Nothing published without a named source. Banned phrases list reviewed annually by counsel.

**Why.** The reputational downside of one unsupportable claim exceeds the upside of all the supportable ones, and it is now directly illegal in the EEA.

**Here.** EU Directive 2024/825 (Empowering Consumers for the Green Transition) takes full effect on 27 September 2026 and blacklists offset-based 'carbon neutral', 'climate neutral', 'CO2 neutral certified', 'climate compensated' and similar claims outright, regardless of credit standard, with penalties up to 4% of annual turnover. Iceland is bound through the EEA. Forward-looking claims ('net zero by 2030') are allowed only with verifiable, independently checked implementation plans.

#### What the lot can honestly say — the claims that survive scrutiny

Defensible: 'all heating from geothermal district heat'; 'no fossil fuel combustion in any permanent building'; 'grid-supplied stage power, with GOs retired against 100% of consumption'; 'location-based grid intensity under 35 gCO2e/kWh'; specific, measured diversion and reuse tonnages. Indefensible: 'carbon neutral', 'zero carbon', '100% renewable' without retired GOs, 'the world's greenest studio'.

**Why.** A studio's sustainability page is read by production sustainability coordinators whose job is to check it. Overclaiming loses the booking; precise underclaiming wins it.

**Here.** The strongest honest claim available is comparative and specific: a scripted drama shooting here displaces the fuel that was 58% of the Sustainable Production Alliance's scripted-TV footprint, because stage power comes from a 99.97% low-carbon grid and heat comes from the ground. Quantify that per shoot day against the production's home territory and let the number do the work.

### Expected

#### On-lot HV ring main and stage substations

Ring-fed HV loop with 2 incoming feeders, ring main units at each stage, and dry-type cast-resin transformers (est. 1,600–2,500 kVA per 2,000 m² stage). Automatic changeover; no single point of failure across four stages.

**Why.** A radial spur means one cable fault dark-holds four stages at once; a ring keeps three shooting while the fourth is isolated.

**Here.** Seismic zone: transformers, switchgear and battery racks need seismic restraint and flexible terminations, not the standard Northern European anchor detail. Ash ingress means outdoor switchgear enclosures need IP55+ and filtered ventilation.

#### Dual-reported grid emission factor, published per stage

Report both: location-based (Iceland's physical mix — 72% hydro, 28% geothermal, 99.97% low-carbon; est. 20–35 gCO2e/kWh, with 34 gCO2/kWh cited for Iceland in 2012 and 5.7 gCO2e/kWh for binary plants) and market-based (GO-backed ~0, or residual 505.51).

**Why.** GHG Protocol Scope 2 requires dual reporting. A studio that publishes only one number will be caught by any competent production sustainability coordinator.

**Here.** Geothermal is not zero-carbon: it vents CO2 and H2S from the reservoir. Orkuveita Reykjavíkur reported 8.7 kt of H2S from Nesjavellir and Hellisheiði in 2022 and has been scaling capture from 25% toward 95% of CO2 and 99% of H2S. Say 'near-zero', never 'zero'.

#### Peak demand (afl) management and load scheduling

Target est. 25–35% diversity across four stages. Automated load shedding of deferrable loads — tank heating, snow melt, EV charging, battery charging, render farm — against a rolling peak. Stage lighting never sheds.

**Why.** Capacity charges are billed on the worst 30 minutes of the month; one careless night of simultaneous pre-light across four stages sets the tariff for a year.

**Here.** Capacity is the scarce commodity in Iceland, not energy. A lot that demonstrably shaves peak is a lot Veitur will connect; a lot that does not is one that waits.

#### Total electrification — no combustion anywhere on the permanent lot

No gas, no oil, no propane in any building. Electric or geothermal heat, induction catering, electric forklifts, scissor lifts, telehandlers and shuttles. Combustion permitted only in effects rigs and the resilience gensets.

**Why.** Every litre of fuel burned on site is Scope 1 for the studio and appears on every tenant production's report.

**Here.** Easy here and hard almost everywhere else: Iceland already heats and cooks electrically/geothermally. This is the claim the lot can make honestly on day one.

#### Rooftop photovoltaics — specified honestly or not at all

Est. 700–900 kWh/kWp/yr at 64°N with an optimal tilt, versus ~950–1,050 in Britain and ~1,600 in Georgia. December yield is effectively nil. Compare: Sky Studios Elstree has >13,000 m² of rooftop PV supplying ~20% of site power; Bottle Yard TBY2 runs a 1 MWp, 2,380-panel array.

**Why.** PV on an Icelandic stage roof is a poster, not a power station — and claiming otherwise is the kind of thing that gets a studio's sustainability page quoted back at it.

**Here.** Generation peaks in June when the backlot has 21 hours of daylight and needs no lighting, and vanishes in December when the lot runs entirely on artificial light. Steep pitch or vertical facade mounting sheds snow and catches low sun; volcanic ash soiling adds a cleaning regime. Honest position: install a modest demonstrator, meter it publicly, and lead the sustainability story with the grid and the heat instead.

#### A written no-genset policy with the tariff to back it

Standard licence term: stage and backlot power is supplied from the grid tie-in and metered; diesel generators are permitted only by exception, logged, and charged at a premium. Publish the kWh rate so productions can budget it against their own genset hire.

**Why.** Productions default to gensets because it is the known cost. Price the alternative transparently and the default flips.

**Here.** Iceland raised its carbon tax by 59% in 2025 (OECD Effective Carbon Rates 2025); explicit carbon prices average EUR 21.01/tCO2e with fuel excise at EUR 75.7/tCO2e. EU ETS2 covering road transport and buildings now starts in 2028 after a March 2026 decision. Diesel gets steadily more expensive here by policy.

#### EV charging infrastructure at crew scale

Est. 60–120 AC points at 7–22 kW across crew and cast parking, plus est. 4–8 DC points at 100–180 kW for trucks and the studio fleet. Load-managed against the campus peak. Open-protocol (OCPP) back office with per-user billing.

**Why.** A thousand-person national crew base means imported HODs in hire cars and a domestic crew increasingly in EVs; a lot without charging forces petrol hire cars into everyone's Scope 3.

**Here.** Iceland has one of the highest EV shares in Europe and a capital-region crew that commutes 15 minutes. Cold-weather range loss makes destination charging at the workplace disproportionately valuable. Specify heated cable management and snow-clear bollard spacing.

#### Electrified studio fleet and plant

Electric shuttles, gators, forklifts, scissor lifts, telehandlers, sweepers, grounds equipment. Where electric plant does not exist (heavy telehandlers, tractors), HVO with a documented supply chain.

**Why.** Studio-owned plant is Scope 1 and the one part of the footprint the lot cannot blame on a tenant.

**Here.** Volcanic ash and salt-laden wind are hard on battery plant; specify sealed motors and rinse-down regimes. Trilith's adjacent Town runs ~600 homes on ground-source geothermal — worth citing as precedent for a studio campus taking its own energy infrastructure seriously.

#### albert Studio Sustainability Standard accreditation

BAFTA albert's facility-level standard, developed with Arup. Six areas: Climate, Circularity, Nature, People, Management, Data. 2025/26 cycle: 31 studios participating, average 77% (up from 70% in 2024), international average 73%; 3 rated Outstanding, 22 Very Good or above. Next applications open 30 September 2026.

**Why.** It is the only global, comparable, independently scored measure of a studio facility. A new lot without it is asserting rather than demonstrating.

**Here.** Benchmark to beat: Bottle Yard Studios TBY2, rated Outstanding at 97%, the highest of 31 global studios. TBY1 scored 83% (Excellent) and Liverpool's The Depot 80% (Very Good). Studios scored well on mental health programmes (97%) and cycle parking (94%) — neither is expensive, and both are within reach on a new build.

#### Green Film certification support

Trentino Film Commission's pan-European rating system, adopted by film commissions and funds across Europe. Two mandatory prerequisites (a sustainability plan and a mobility plan) plus a points score; the current edition scores out of 65 with a minimum of 25 to certify. Verified by independent audit.

**Why.** It is the only system certifying international co-productions under one procedure across every European country — which is what an Icelandic lot's European clients will be doing.

**Here.** Iceland's business is heavily European co-production. Green Film compliance is increasingly tied to national fund access elsewhere in Europe; the Icelandic rebate has no green condition yet, but the European money arriving alongside it frequently does.

#### PGA Green / PEAR / EMA Green Seal support for US clients

Green Production Guide's PEACH checklist (which doubles as the EMA Green Seal application, USD 1,000 fee) and the PEAR carbon calculator, plus a searchable vendor database of 2,000+ eco-vendors. Maintain an Icelandic vendor list mapped to it.

**Why.** American studios and streamers run on PGA Green paperwork, not albert. A lot fluent in only one dialect halves its addressable market.

**Here.** The 2,000-vendor PGA database contains almost nothing in Iceland. Building and publishing a verified Icelandic equivalent — caterers, waste, timber, hire, transport — is genuinely valuable and nobody else has done it.

#### AdGreen support for commercials and stills

AdGreen's free carbon calculator covers motion, stills and audio anywhere in the world, with Early Insights estimates usable at bid stage.

**Why.** Commercials fill the gaps between features and are the lot's cash-flow floor; agencies increasingly require AdGreen data at bid.

**Here.** Iceland's car-commercial and fashion-stills traffic is large relative to its drama volume. Bid-stage carbon estimating is exactly when a client decides between Iceland and somewhere closer.

#### BREEAM New Construction v7 (or Nordic Swan) certification of the build

BREEAM NC v7 came into force September 2025 with far greater weight on whole-life carbon, operational performance, biodiversity, resilience and material selection. Target Excellent as the floor, Outstanding on the office/production buildings.

**Why.** It is the standard the comparable new lots were built to and the one an international client recognises without explanation.

**Here.** BREEAM and the Nordic Swan Ecolabel are the two systems actually used to certify buildings in Iceland; Grænni byggð is the Icelandic green building council. Benchmarks: Sky Studios Elstree achieved post-completion BREEAM Excellent with buildings rated Excellent to Outstanding; Shinfield Studios was built to BREEAM Excellent with twin-wall structure, air-source heat pumps and PV; Sunset Waltham Cross targets BREEAM Excellent and LEED Gold across 21 stages on 91 acres.

#### Low-carbon structural specification with EPDs

Environmental Product Declarations (EN 15804) required for steel, concrete, cladding and insulation. Electric-arc-furnace steel with high recycled content; GGBS/limestone-calcined-clay cement replacement; specify by performance at 56 or 90 days rather than 28 to allow lower-clinker mixes.

**Why.** Structure and substructure dominate embodied carbon; specifying by EPD is the only way to hold the number through value engineering.

**Here.** Iceland has no domestic steel and imports its cement, so EPDs come with a real freight leg attached. Icelandic volcanic pozzolans and imported GGBS are both worth testing. Rebar and section from Nordic EAF mills (est. 400–700 kgCO2e/t) versus blast-furnace imports (est. 1,800–2,300 kgCO2e/t) is the largest single specification decision on the project.

#### Freight carbon of the build, counted and minimised

Count A4 (transport to site) explicitly. Consolidate into full shiploads from one or two European ports, sequence to avoid air freight entirely, and write a contractual air-freight ban with named exceptions.

**Why.** 'Everything structural is imported' means A4 is a material line, not a rounding error — and one emergency air-freighted shipment can exceed a year of operational emissions.

**Here.** Sea freight to Iceland is weather-dependent and schedules slip, which is precisely what tempts a contractor into air freight. Build float into the programme as a carbon control, not just a cost control. Samskip's hydrogen-powered SeaShuttles enter service from early 2027, each expected to cut ~25,000 t CO2/yr in zero-emission mode — worth tracking as a future low-carbon lane.

#### Refrigerant strategy

Low-GWP refrigerants throughout (GWP <150 where possible, <750 as the ceiling), leak detection on every charge above est. 50 kg, annual leak testing, recorded top-ups reported as Scope 1. No new equipment on high-GWP HFCs.

**Why.** Refrigerant leakage is the quietest large Scope 1 line on any campus and the one nobody measures until an auditor asks.

**Here.** Iceland applies EU F-gas rules through the EEA; the 2024 recast tightens the phase-down and bans on new equipment. Specify to the future rule, not the current one, because the plant will outlive it.

#### Biodiversity, land and revegetation across 809 hectares

Baseline ecological survey before ground-breaking; measurable net gain target; revegetation with native species; protection of nesting birds and any wetland. BREEAM v7 and LEED v5 both now weight biodiversity and resilience heavily.

**Why.** 809 hectares is a landholding, not a plot. Managed badly it is a liability; managed well it is the lot's most distinctive asset and a genuine carbon sink.

**Here.** Iceland's Long-Term Low Emission Development Strategy leans specifically on afforestation, revegetation and wetland reclamation. Ground-nesting birds (golden plover, whimbrel, snipe, arctic tern) constrain the construction season and backlot activity between roughly May and July — that is a scheduling fact for the backlot, not just an ecology note. Wetland drainage reversal on unused land is a credible, locally-appropriate removal.

#### Set strike protocol and construction waste control

Contractual strike plan submitted before build starts: what is reused, what is returned to the library, what is recycled, what is residual — with weights reported at wrap. Deposit held against the diversion rate. No skip leaves unweighed.

**Why.** The strike is where a production's waste footprint is actually decided, and it happens under time pressure when nobody is watching.

**Here.** Disposal capacity in the capital region is finite and SORPA's landfill is winding down; a 13,200 m² lot producing unsorted strike waste will become a named problem locally very quickly.

#### Paint, chemicals, batteries and e-waste

COSHH-equivalent store, paint take-back and remix service, solvent recovery, battery and WEEE collection points on every stage. Hazardous waste consigned and documented under Icelandic regulation.

**Why.** These are the streams that create pollution incidents and regulatory exposure, and paint in particular is a large, recoverable volume.

**Here.** Iceland's producer-responsibility fund (Úrvinnslusjóður) covers recycling fees on imported goods and packaging; budget it as an import cost line. The hazardous incinerator is the only thermal route in the country.

#### Rainwater, snowmelt harvesting and surface water management

Harvest from est. 20,000–30,000 m² of roof. Reykjavík receives ~800 mm of precipitation across ~150 precipitation days a year; Mosfellsbær's wettest month, February, sees ~154 mm and the driest, June, ~89 mm. Attenuation and SuDS sized for cloudburst plus rapid thaw. Use for effects water, tank top-up, irrigation and washdown.

**Why.** Uncontrolled surface water on 809 hectares of Icelandic ground will find the stage doors; and effects water does not need to be potable.

**Here.** The design case is not drought, it is rain-on-snow and rapid thaw with saturated or frozen ground. Size attenuation for a thaw event, not a summer storm. Harvested water for effects and tank top-up is genuinely useful; harvesting for potable use is pointless where mains water is pristine and cheap.

#### Sustainability function — staffed, budgeted, and inside operations

A permanent Head of Sustainability reporting to the Studio Operations Director, not to marketing. Est. 2–3 FTE covering energy/carbon data, waste and circularity, and production liaison. Annual budget est. EUR 400k–900k excluding capital.

**Why.** Every item above is a process, not a purchase. Unowned, they decay into a certificate on a wall within two years of opening.
