**Here.** The national crew base is about a thousand people and this role does not exist in Iceland yet — it will be an import, housed, like the heads of department. Budget accordingly, and use it as a training pipeline: an Icelandic-trained production sustainability cohort is a competitive asset before the rebate expires at the end of 2028.

### State of the art

#### ISO 50001 energy management system with an accredited certificate

Certified EnMS covering the whole campus: energy baseline, significant energy uses, EnPIs (kWh/m²/occupied-stage-day, kWh per shoot day), annual internal audit, management review, external surveillance.

**Why.** It turns energy from a bill into a managed process, and it is the evidence base every certification scheme asks for.

**Here.** Few Icelandic facilities bother because energy is cheap — which is exactly why it is a differentiator for an international client whose parent reports under CSRD.

#### Return-water (bakvatn) cascade and snow melting

Capture <30 °C return water at ISK 6.60/m³ (7.47 incl. VAT) — 28× cheaper than fresh supply — and cascade it: stage heating → tank top-up → snow melt loops under roads, unit-base hardstanding and stage door aprons. Dedicated snow-melt tariff S1: ISK 767.95/day standing + 106.55/m³ + 0.23 kr/m²/day.

**Why.** Snow-melted access roads and stage aprons are the difference between a call time held and a call time lost, and on return water the heat is nearly free.

**Here.** Snjóbræðsla is standard Icelandic civic practice, not a luxury. Mosfellsbær sits on the Reykir low-temperature field that historically fed Reykjavík — proximity to the resource is a genuine site advantage. Note the VAT trap: separately metered snow melt carries 24% VAT, not 11%.

#### Heat recovery from stages, LED volume and render/post

Plate or run-around heat recovery on stage extract, plus water-cooled racks in the render room. Est. 150–400 kW of recoverable low-grade heat when two stages and the volume are live; route to tank top-up and DHW rather than reject it.

**Why.** In a country where heat is cheap, recovery pays back on carbon and on capacity, not on cost — it shrinks the connected load you have to reserve.

**Here.** The economic case is weak (heat costs ISK 92–183/m³); the capacity case is strong. Frame it as load avoidance, which is the currency that matters to Veitur and Landsnet.

#### On-site wind generation

Est. 1–3 turbines of 0.9–4 MW on the 809 ha, sited for wake and shadow-flicker separation. Landsvirkjun's two 0.9 MW research turbines at Hafið are rated at a combined output up to 5.4 GWh/yr. Búrfellslundur: 28 turbines, ~17 km², >120 MW, first power 2026, full operation 2027.

**Why.** Behind-the-meter generation is the only way to add real energy on a grid that will not sell you more, and it turns the windiest liability on the site into the asset.

**Here.** Iceland is among the windiest inhabited places on earth and Landsvirkjun describes conditions as highly favourable. But: icing, extreme gusts and rime loading demand cold-climate-package turbines with blade heating; planning and public opposition to onshore wind are genuine and slow; and noise/flicker must clear the stages and any housing. Also check whether export back to the grid is even permitted under your connection agreement.

#### Campus battery energy storage system (BESS)

Est. 2–6 MW / 4–12 MWh lithium iron phosphate in a fire-rated, bunded, seismically restrained outdoor enclosure. Functions: peak shaving against the capacity charge, ride-through for the volume and render farm, black-start of critical loads, and charging the mobile fleet overnight.

**Why.** It is the mechanism that lets a modest grid connection serve a lot with four stages of spiky lighting load, and it is the alternative to a bigger connection you cannot get.

**Here.** LFP over NMC for fire safety and cold tolerance; specify cell heating for sub-zero charging. Seismic qualification (IEEE 693 or equivalent) is not optional here. Cross-check the fire strategy with the Icelandic fire authority — BESS guidance in Iceland is thin, so expect to import NFPA 855 or equivalent as the basis of design.

#### Microgrid controller and intentional islanding

Controller that can island the campus on loss of grid and re-synchronise without interruption, sequencing BESS, wind, PV and gensets. Target: zero-interruption transfer for stages and volume, sub-10-minute cold-start for the whole lot.

**Why.** A take mid-shot is worth more than the whole switchgear. Islanding is how you turn a rural, weather-exposed grid spur into a facility a studio will insure a schedule against.

**Here.** Storms regularly bring down Icelandic distribution, and the site is on a spur 15 minutes from Reykjavík. Islanding capability is a selling point that a British or Georgian lot does not need to advertise.

#### Mobile battery power units in place of tow-behind diesel

Fleet of mobile BESS for unit bases, backlot and location. Reference products: Moxion MP-75/600 (75 kW / 600 kWh, deployed by Amazon on US productions); Portable Electric Voltstack 30k and Voltstack 8000 (8 kW continuous, 16 kW surge, ~10 kWh). Est. fleet 6–10 units, 2–4 MWh total.

**Why.** Fuel was ~48% of a tentpole's footprint and 58% of a scripted TV drama's in the Sustainable Production Alliance's Close Up report. Displacing it is the largest single carbon lever a lot controls.

**Here.** Silent operation matters more here than most places: Icelandic locations are acoustically dead and exteriors are frequently the point. Cold-weather derating is real — specify heated packs. Note the vendor market is thin and consolidating (est.), so avoid single-source dependency and keep the units serviceable locally.

#### Distributed lot power — 'shore power' pedestals across the backlot and unit bases

Weatherproof, metered pedestals at every unit base, circus position, backlot standing set and crane pad. Est. 63 A / 125 A / 400 A three-phase outlets on the 809 ha, fed from the ring main. IP67, heated, sub-metered per outlet.

**Why.** A tie-in that exists but is 400 m from where the trucks park is a tie-in nobody uses. Pedestals are what actually retire the generators.

**Here.** Ducting across 809 ha of Icelandic ground means frost depth, lava substrate and seismic movement joints — cost it as civils, not as electrics. Trenching windows are short: est. May–September.

#### The lot as a power vendor — rentable clean power kit

In-house hire catalogue: mobile BESS, distro, cable runs, LED lighting packages, EV vans. Priced alongside stage hire. LEDs draw up to 80–85% less than tungsten equivalents.

**Why.** Productions will only take the clean option if it is one phone call and one invoice. Owning the kit also captures margin a hire house would otherwise take.

**Here.** There is no deep local grip/lighting hire market and everything imported arrives by weather-dependent sea freight. Owning the kit is not a green initiative here — it is the difference between a production being able to shoot and not.

#### Automated production carbon data feed

Portal where a licensed production sees its own stage-level electricity, heat, water and waste in near-real time, exportable in albert, PEAR and Green Film formats. Est. build cost EUR 80–200k plus metering.

**Why.** Carbon data is currently gathered by a coordinator with a clipboard at wrap. Automating it is the single most-requested thing productions cannot get from any lot.

**Here.** This is the differentiator available to a new build with no legacy metering. Sky Studios Elstree, Bottle Yard and Shinfield all retrofitted; a greenfield lot can meter everything from the first pour.

#### Third-party verification and assurance

Annual limited assurance over the GHG inventory to ISO 14064-3 by an accredited body; escalate to reasonable assurance on Scopes 1 and 2 once stable. Publish the assurance statement, not just the number.

**Why.** An unverified number is a marketing claim. Verified numbers survive procurement, due diligence and journalism.

**Here.** Assurance capacity exists in Iceland through the Big Four Reykjavík offices and Icelandic engineering consultancies (EFLA, Verkís, Mannvit), all of whom already work on Icelandic energy and climate projects.

#### Science-based target and regulatory reporting exposure

SBTi-validated near-term and net-zero targets for the operating company. Map exposure to CSRD/ESRS through the Icelandic transposition, and to EU ETS2 (MRV obligations began 2025; scheme start postponed to 2028).

**Why.** The owner is a production company with a family fortune behind it; the moment it takes institutional money or a large studio partner, unverified targets become a diligence problem.

**Here.** Iceland is bound through the EEA. National targets: −55% by 2030 versus 2005 under the Effort Sharing Regulation, a 50–55% 2035 NDC, and climate neutrality before 2040 written into Climate Act No. 70/2012 as amended in 2021. Samskip, the Icelandic shipping line, already holds SBTi-validated targets (−50% by 2030, net zero 2040), so the local precedent exists.

#### Offsets and removals policy — and the discipline not to lead with it

Residual-only, removals-preferred, permanence-weighted. Iceland has the world's best removal options: Carbfix's Coda Terminal at Straumsvík is designed for 3 Mt CO2/yr at full scale (operations from mid-2026, full capacity 2031), and Climeworks' Mammoth DAC plant at Hellisheiði captures up to 36,000 t/yr into basalt.

**Why.** Offsetting is now a compliance liability as well as a credibility one, and mineralised storage is the only kind that is unarguably permanent.

**Here.** Two warnings. First, Kolviður, the Icelandic afforestation fund, does not hold formally registered certified credits and does not meet ICROA/GHG Protocol/Verra/ISO 14064 or the Icelandic ÍST TS 2022:92 criteria — do not buy it and call it an offset. Second, albert now requires productions to offset unavoidable emissions for the logo, so the lot should broker quality supply rather than let tenants buy the cheapest thing available.

#### LEED v5 certification on at least the client-facing buildings

LEED v5 released April 2025: nearly 50% of points now tied to carbon (embodied, operational, refrigerants, transport), with a new embodied-carbon quantification prerequisite and mandatory life-cycle assessment.

**Why.** American studios read LEED and do not read BREEAM. Dual certification is expensive but it is the price of speaking to both halves of the market.

**Here.** Dual certification on the same building is est. 15–30% more consultant cost than one scheme, and much of the evidence base overlaps. Certify the production office, post building and mill to LEED; BREEAM the stages.

#### Whole-life carbon assessment of the build itself

EN 15978 / RICS Whole Life Carbon Assessment covering modules A1–A5, B, C and D, at concept, at technical design and as-built. Benchmarks: LETI's 2030 target is 350 kgCO2e/m² (A1–A5) for commercial; RIBA 2030 sets 500; a distribution-warehouse baseline case measured 234 kgCO2e/m², with frame and upper floors at 32.

**Why.** A steel-framed 30 m-clear stage is a warehouse with a heavier frame and a heavier roof; without a measured target the structural engineer will optimise for programme and price alone.

**Here.** Est. for this lot: 13,200 m² of stage plus support at roughly 300–450 kgCO2e/m², so an order of 6,000–12,000 tCO2e embodied for the stages alone before infrastructure, roads and the tank — comfortably more than a decade of operational emissions on a near-zero-carbon grid. This inverts the usual priority: at Sugar Land, embodied carbon IS the carbon story.

#### Design for disassembly and a material passport

Bolted rather than welded primary connections where practical, demountable cladding, no composite bonded panels that cannot be separated. A digital material passport recording every product, quantity, EPD and disassembly route.

**Why.** Module D benefit is real, and a lot built to be taken apart is a lot that can be reconfigured when a stage's use changes — which it will.

**Here.** Nothing gets demolished cheaply on an island where disposal capacity is small and export of waste is expensive. Designing for recovery is an operating cost decision here, not just a carbon one.

#### Set store and scenery library — the lot owns the flats

Est. 2,000–4,000 m² heated, racked storage. Studio-owned stock of standard-module flattage, rostra, stairs, doors and windows, barcoded into a searchable catalogue, hired by the week rather than built and skipped. Standard flat sizes published so productions design to them.

**Why.** albert reports a tentpole ($70M+) uses enough plywood to fill 2.5 cargo planes, and the average show or movie generated about 240 tonnes of waste in 2022. Reuse is the only lever that touches that at source.

**Here.** On an island where every sheet of ply arrives by sea, a hire library is straightforwardly cheaper for the production, not just greener. This is the item where Iceland's freight disadvantage becomes a commercial argument for circularity — it may be the strongest sustainability story the lot has.

#### Materials exchange and donation route

Post-strike materials made available to schools, theatres, community groups and other productions, with a logged chain of custody and weights. Model: EcoSet's Materials Oasis in Los Angeles, which has catalysed reuse of more than 145 tonnes and supplies non-profits free of charge; EcoSet has worked with Netflix and HBO since 2009.

**Why.** It converts a disposal cost into a community relationship and generates the documented diversion tonnage certification schemes require.

**Here.** Iceland is small enough that this can genuinely work — a national network of schools, theatres and the Reykjavík art scene is reachable in one afternoon. It also addresses the political problem of a foreign-funded studio on family land in Mosfellsbær.

#### Tank water treatment chemistry and reuse policy

Low-chlorine or chlorine-free primary treatment (UV, ozone, or ozone/peroxide) with residual sanitiser only as required. Continuous monitoring of free chlorine, pH, ORP, turbidity and temperature, logged. Written policy: water is treated and held between productions, not drained and refilled.

**Why.** Refilling 4.8 million litres between every booking is the largest avoidable water and heat footprint on the lot; holding it treated is cheaper and reportable.

**Here.** Chlorinating pristine Icelandic groundwater, then discharging it, is a poor look in a country that does not chlorinate its drinking water. UV and ozone are the defensible choices. Note that ozone plant is an electrical load (est. 30–80 kW) — feed it from the deferrable-load list.

### What gets got wrong

- THE ICELAND CARBON PARADOX. Iceland's physical grid is 99.97% low-carbon (72% hydro, 28% geothermal, 19.06 TWh in 2024) but its PAPER grid is filthy. Because Icelandic producers sell renewable Guarantees of Origin into Europe, AIB's 2024 residual mix for Iceland is 505.51 gCO2/kWh — 71.22% fossil, 18.26% nuclear, 77.80% untracked — against a European residual average around 435. Iceland drew 14.05 TWh of attributes FROM the European Attribute Mix in 2024, roughly its whole consumption. A production reporting market-based Scope 2 in Iceland without retired GOs reports a worse number than it would get in Britain. Buying and cancelling Icelandic GOs costs est. EUR 2-8/MWh and is the cheapest, highest-leverage line on the entire sustainability budget. Get it wrong and the lot's central selling point inverts.
- THE GRID IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, NOT THE PRICE. Landsvirkjun has said the system is at full capacity, that it will not prioritise new large-scale industrial customers, and it refused supply to hydrogen and e-fuel developers — prompting an ESA competition investigation in April 2025. Cheap electricity you cannot get is worth nothing. Secure the connection agreement and reserved MVA before any other decision, and present the lot as commercial distribution load, not stóriðja.
- EMBODIED CARBON DWARFS OPERATIONAL CARBON HERE, AND THE USUAL PRIORITIES INVERT. On a near-zero-carbon grid with geothermal heat, operational emissions are small. The est. 6,000-12,000 tCO2e embodied in 13,200 m² of imported steel-framed stages is more than a decade of operations. Everything structural arrives by sea. If the sustainability programme is built around operational energy — the default everywhere else — it optimises the small number and ignores the large one.
- NON-PRIORITY HOT WATER IS INTERRUPTIBLE BY CONTRACT. Veitur's H3, H4 and V1-V5 tariffs are explicitly 'ekki forgangsvatn' with no discount for curtailment. The tank stage on the H4 pool tariff (ISK 91.63/m³) can legally lose its heat in a cold snap, and Veitur's peak hot-water demand grows ~120 L/s a year with new wells at Kjalarnes and Geldinganes 3-5 years out. Either negotiate a priority allocation or fit an electric backup exchanger — a 4.8 million-litre tank cooling from 30 °C mid-shoot is a schedule-ending event.
- ICELAND'S WASTEWATER INFRASTRUCTURE WILL NOT ACCEPT YOUR PROBLEM. The capital region's plants, largest being Veitur's Klettagarðar, give mechanical/preliminary treatment only before ocean discharge; secondary treatment is minimal nationally and Veitur holds an exemption. A 2,400 m² tank drain-down, or effects water carrying fuller's earth, paint or fuel residue, must be treated on site. Assume an on-site treatment plant, not a connection.
- SOLAR IS A POSTER, NOT A POWER STATION. At 64°N, est. 700-900 kWh/kWp/yr with essentially nil output in December — exactly when the backlot runs entirely on artificial light — and peak output in June when 21 hours of daylight means the lot needs none. Sky Studios Elstree gets ~20% of site power from 13,000 m² of PV; the same array here would get a fraction of that. Install a metered demonstrator, publish the real yield, and lead the story with the grid and the heat.
- KOLVIÐUR IS NOT AN OFFSET. Iceland's afforestation fund does not hold formally registered certified credits and does not meet ICROA, GHG Protocol, Verra, ISO 14064 or the Icelandic ÍST TS 2022:92 criteria. Buying it and reporting it as an offset is a documented greenwash. The credible Icelandic routes are Carbfix mineralisation (Coda Terminal, 3 Mt/yr at full scale from 2031) and Climeworks Mammoth DAC (up to 36,000 t/yr) — real, permanent, and expensive.
- 'CARBON NEUTRAL' BECOMES ILLEGAL IN THE EEA ON 27 SEPTEMBER 2026. Directive (EU) 2024/825 blacklists offset-based claims of neutral, reduced or positive GHG impact — 'climate neutral', 'CO2 neutral certified', 'climate compensated' — regardless of credit standard, with penalties to 4% of turnover. Iceland is bound through the EEA. Every marketing line drafted before that date needs re-clearing.
- ALBERT MOVED THE GOALPOSTS IN 2025. The production certification pass threshold rose from 30% to 55%, the question set widened, and unavoidable emissions must now be offset to receive the logo. A lot whose data provision was adequate in 2023 is not adequate now. albert's facility standard, run with Arup across six areas, had 31 studios averaging 77% in 2025/26 — Bottle Yard's TBY2 set the bar at 97% Outstanding. A new lot entering below the average is a story, not a credential.
- PRODUCTION EMISSIONS ARE NOT THE STUDIO'S EMISSIONS UNTIL YOU SAY THEY ARE. Decide the inventory boundary in writing on day one and publish tenant production emissions as a separate disclosed category. Reporting a tiny campus footprint while four stages burn is the sector's most common and most easily exposed greenwash.
- GEOTHERMAL IS NOT ZERO-CARBON. It vents CO2 and H2S from the reservoir — Orkuveita Reykjavíkur reported 8.7 kt of H2S from Nesjavellir and Hellisheiði in 2022, with capture scaling from 25% toward 95% of CO2. Icelandic geothermal electricity has been cited at 34 gCO2/kWh; district heat at 5.8 kg CO2e/MWh delivered. Say 'near-zero', never 'zero', and never claim the heat is free of emissions.
- HVO MAY NOT ACTUALLY BE BUYABLE IN ICELAND. Specifying renewable diesel for the standby gensets and heavy plant is standard UK practice, but I could not confirm that N1, Olís, Orkan or Atlantsolía stock it. Verify supply before it appears in the sustainability plan; imported drums at Icelandic freight rates are a different proposition entirely, and an unsupportable fuel claim is worse than an honest fossil one.
- THE FREIGHT DISADVANTAGE IS THE CIRCULARITY ARGUMENT. Everything arrives by weather-dependent sea. That makes a studio-owned scenery library, standard flat modules and a materials exchange cheaper for the production, not just greener — albert reckons a $70M+ tentpole uses enough plywood to fill 2.5 cargo planes, and the average show generated ~240 tonnes of waste in 2022. This is probably the strongest sustainability story the lot has, and it is a commercial argument first.
- BUILD PROGRAMME SLIP CAUSES AIR FREIGHT, WHICH CAUSES THE EMBODIED CARBON TO BLOW. Write a contractual air-freight ban with named exceptions and build sea-freight float into the programme as a carbon control, not only a cost control. One emergency air shipment can exceed a year of operational emissions.
- GROUND-NESTING BIRDS AND THE TRENCHING WINDOW ARE SCHEDULING FACTS. Golden plover, whimbrel, snipe and tern constrain construction and backlot activity roughly May to July, which is also the only workable civils window (est. May-September) for 809 hectares of ducting and drainage. The ecology constraint and the buildability constraint sit on top of each other.
- SEISMIC AND ASH ARE DESIGN INPUTS FOR THE ENERGY PLANT, NOT FOOTNOTES. Transformers, switchgear and battery racks need seismic restraint and flexible terminations; outdoor enclosures need IP55+ with filtered ventilation against volcanic ash. Icelandic guidance on battery storage fire safety is thin — import NFPA 855 or equivalent as the basis of design and agree it with the fire authority early.
- THE REBATE EXPIRES AT THE END OF 2028 AND HAS NO GREEN CONDITION — YET. Act No. 43/1999 sunsets 31 December 2028. European co-production money arriving alongside it increasingly does carry green conditions (Green Film is the only system certifying international co-productions under one procedure). Build the sustainability data capability now so that if a green condition is attached to the renewal, the lot is already compliant rather than scrambling.
- IF YOU CANNOT METER IT, NO PRODUCTION CAN REPORT IT. Half-hourly, stage-level, API-exposed metering of electricity, heat, water and weighed waste is the entire foundation. A greenfield lot can install it from the first pour, which Sky Studios Elstree, Shinfield and Bottle Yard all had to retrofit. Skipping it to save est. EUR 150-300k costs the lot every albert, Green Film and PEAR certification its tenants try to earn.

---

## 10. Planning, permits and the neighbours

*Sugar Land Studios, Mosfellsbær, Iceland (campus/lot scale)*

### Essential

#### Aðalskipulag amendment: agricultural land to a studio designation

Skipulagslög 123/2010 arts. 30–32. Farmland (landbúnaðarsvæði, L) must be re-designated, likely to athafnasvæði (AT) or iðnaðarsvæði (I). Lýsing first, then ≥6 weeks public advertisement (art. 31), council resolution, agency confirmation ~4 weeks. Estimate 14–20 months to staðfesting.

**Why.** Build on an agricultural designation and every downstream permit is void; a deiliskipulag cannot lawfully depart from the aðalskipulag above it.

**Here.** Mosfellsbær is 186 km2 with 13,715 people (2025). 809 ha is 4.3% of the whole municipality's land area — this is a municipality-defining decision, not a routine amendment.

#### Lýsing (statutory scoping statement) for both plan levels

Skipulagslög 123/2010 art. 30 (aðalskipulag) and art. 40 (deiliskipulag). A public scoping document stating objectives, premises, existing policy and the consultation route, issued to the planning agency and consultees before any draft plan. The deiliskipulag lýsing may be waived if the aðalskipulag settles all premises.

**Why.** Omitting it invalidates the plan on procedure alone and is among the commonest grounds on which an Icelandic consent is quashed.

#### Deiliskipulag (site-level detailed plan) drawn oversize

Arts. 37–42. Fixes byggingarreitir (building envelopes), hámarkshæð, nýtingarhlutfall (plot ratio per ÍST 50), access, parking, services, noise and lighting policy. ≥6 weeks advertisement; the council must submit the adopted plan to the agency within 6 months (art. 42).

**Why.** A 30 m stage, a 2,400 m2 tank and a backlot need envelopes that will still fit the fifth building nobody has designed yet.

**Here.** Write named phases and a 15-year horizon into one plan so phases 2–4 do not each need a fresh ≥6-week advertisement cycle.

#### Svæðisskipulag höfuðborgarsvæðisins conformity and the growth boundary

Mosfellsbær is bound by the capital-region regional plan (Svæðisskipulag höfuðborgarsvæðisins 2015–2040) as well as its own. ESTIMATE/VERIFY: the regional plan operates a growth boundary (vaxtarmörk) separating urban from rural land, and development outside it needs regional-committee agreement.

**Why.** The regional plan can veto a municipality that has already said yes. It is the likeliest hard stop on the whole project.

**Here.** Could not verify the current boundary text from here. This is item one of legal due diligence, before land is committed.

#### Correct addressee: Skipulagsstofnun's functions now sit with HMS

skipulag.is redirects to island.is and the agency page is branded Húsnæðis-, mannvirkja- og skipulagsstofnun (HMS) as of 2026. Verify the vesting date and exactly which statutory opinions HMS now issues under 123/2010 and 111/2021 before filing anything.

**Why.** Filing a statutory submission with an agency that no longer exists burns a consultation window you cannot get back.

**Here.** Iceland reorganised environmental agencies around 1 Jan 2025 — Umhverfisstofnun's functions now appear under Umhverfis- og orkustofnun and Náttúruverndarstofnun. Re-check every addressee.

#### EIA is mandatory, not screened — Category A

Lög 111/2021, Viðauki 1 item 10.01: industrial development of ≥20,000 m2 site or building floor area is flokkur A, where assessment is automatic. 13,200 m2 of stage plus workshops, offices, post, mill, stores, plant and parking will exceed 20,000 m2 gross (estimate, near-certain).

**Why.** Assuming a screening opinion when the project is automatically Category A loses a year and the applicant's credibility with the regulator.

#### Matsáætlun (assessment plan) scoped to the whole 809 ha

Lög 111/2021: the developer's assessment plan goes on public display for ≥4 weeks; the agency issues its opinion within 7 weeks of complete documentation. Scope it to the full masterplan horizon, not to phase one.

**Why.** A phase-one scope forces an entire second EIA when stage five is proposed, five years later, with the rebate already expired.

#### Umhverfismatsskýrsla (environmental impact statement)

Lög 111/2021: minimum 6-week public consultation, agency opinion within 7 weeks of close. Must carry alternatives including the zero option, weighted impact assessment, mitigation and a monitoring plan. Estimate 24–36 months from matsáætlun to final opinion for a project this size.

**Why.** The monitoring plan becomes enforceable permit conditions. Write it as something the lot can live with for thirty years, not as a promise.

#### The EIA chapters a studio lot specifically needs

Landscape and visual (a 30 m box in an open valley), noise, obtrusive light, traffic, water (abstraction and discharge for a 2,400 m2 tank), archaeology, breeding birds, wetlands, waste, GHG and energy, socio-economic and employment, and cumulative effects.

**Why.** A missing chapter is the cheapest thing for an objector to find and the most expensive thing to bolt on late.

#### Framkvæmdaleyfi (development permit) for everything below ground

Skipulagslög 123/2010 arts. 13–16 and reglugerð 772/2012. Required for earthworks, site roads, the tank excavation, services, drainage and vegetation clearance (772/2012 art. 5). Conditions may impose mitigation, monitoring and safety measures (art. 10). Lapses if work has not started within 12 months (art. 14).

**Why.** Separate from and prior to the building permit. Moving earth without it is an offence and stops the whole site.

#### Byggingarleyfi (building permit) and the Icelandic team requirements

Mannvirkjalög 160/2010 art. 9, from the municipal byggingarfulltrúi. Requires a certified hönnunarstjóri (art. 23) and byggingarstjóri (art. 27) carrying liability insurance for 5 years post-completion (art. 29(8)), plus HMS-registered quality-management systems for designers, contractors and managers (arts. 24, 31, 32).

**Why.** No registered QMS, no permit. Imported design and build teams routinely arrive without Icelandic registration and lose months to it.

**Here.** Everything structural is imported, but the signing roles cannot be. Appoint the Icelandic hönnunarstjóri and byggingarstjóri before, not after, the foreign design team is engaged.

#### Inspection gates and enforcement teeth

160/2010 art. 35 öryggisúttekt (safety inspection) before any occupation or use; art. 36 lokaúttekt (final inspection) within 3 years of occupation. Enforcement: stop-work and closure (art. 55), dagsektir up to ISK 500,000 per day (art. 56), administrative fines up to ISK 10,000,000 (art. 57a, added by L. 113/2023).

**Why.** A stage cannot lawfully host a paying production before öryggisúttekt. Sell dates off that milestone, not off practical completion.

#### Starfsleyfi (operating licences) — plural

Lög 7/1998 art. 6: listed activities need a valid operating licence. Annex I/II from Umhverfis- og orkustofnun, Annex IV from the local heilbrigðisnefnd (art. 7); draft licences are publicly advertised for 4 weeks. Likely triggers: fuel and gas storage, standby generation, spray and paint, workshops, wastewater, catering, water treatment.

**Why.** The lot needs several licences, not one. Each carries its own 4-week public window and its own appeal clock.

**Here.** Heilbrigðiseftirlit Kjósarsvæðis has been the health inspectorate for Mosfellsbær; verify current boundaries after recent reorganisation.

#### Water abstraction, treatment and discharge for the tank

A 2,400 m2 tank at ~3 m working depth is roughly 7,200 m3 per fill (estimate). Abstraction, chemical dosing, filtration and discharge each need consent. Design for closed-loop recirculation and effectively zero discharge, with a lined tank and a bunded plant room.

**Why.** Discharging thousands of cubic metres of dosed water to a salmonid catchment ends a consent and a reputation in the same week.

**Here.** Kaldakvísl drains Mosfellsdalur into the Leirvogur estuary; Leirvogsá is a named angling river. Treat the catchment, not the tank, as the binding constraint on the water stage.

#### Vegagerðin junction authority and road setbacks

Vegalög 80/2007 art. 29: connecting to a þjóðvegur requires Vegagerðin's authority; reglugerð 180/2015 sets minimum junction spacing and design by road class and traffic volume. Art. 32: building setback ≥30 m from centreline for stofnvegir, ≥15 m for other national roads, 40 m at junctions and extendable to 150 m.

**Why.** The junction is usually the long pole, and Vegagerðin can require a grade-separated or signalised solution the developer must fund entirely.

**Here.** Route 36 Þingvallavegur runs the length of Mosfellsdalur; Route 1 Vesturlandsvegur is the trunk connection to Reykjavík. Both are two-lane rural on the relevant stretches.

#### Traffic impact assessment built on counter-peak call times

Estimate for four stages at full occupancy: 400–900 people on site daily, peaking above 1,200 on a big unit day, plus 30–60 HGV movements per day in build weeks. Model AM/PM road peaks against unit call times (06:00 in, 20:00 out), which sit outside the commuter peak.

**Why.** Studios generate counter-peak traffic. Quantifying that converts a road-capacity objection into a road-capacity non-issue.

**Here.** Vegagerðin publishes ÁDU (annual average daily traffic) by counting station — obtain the current figures for Routes 1 and 36 near Mosfellsbær; could not retrieve them here.

#### Abnormal-load route agreed before the plan is fixed

Agree the abnormal-load route from Sundahöfn (Reykjavík) or Grundartangi with Vegagerðin and the municipality before the deiliskipulag geometry is set. Bridge, roundabout and junction geometry on the approach governs maximum member length and transformer mass.

**Why.** Discovering a 24 m truss cannot turn into the site after the steel is ordered costs a whole construction season.

**Here.** Sea freight is weather-dependent. Carry 4–8 weeks of float per major shipment (estimate) and size the laydown yard for the resulting stockpile.

#### The boundary noise limit that sizes the whole plant strategy

Reglugerð 724/2008, Tafla III, at the facade of dwellings: íbúðarsvæði 50 dB(A) day 07–19, 45 dB(A) evening 19–23, 40 dB(A) night 23–07. Verslunar-/þjónustu-/miðsvæði 55/55/40. Iðnaðar- og athafnasvæði 70 dB(A) at all times. Indoors 30 dB(A), 25 dB(A) at night.

**Why.** The 40 dB(A) night figure at the nearest dwelling sizes every silencer, enclosure, bund and plant-yard location on the lot.

#### The 35 dB(A) summer-house trap

Reglugerð 724/2008 sets 35 dB(A) for frístundabyggð (summer-house areas) at all periods — 5 dB tighter than the night limit for permanent housing and 15 dB tighter than its daytime limit. A single lawfully designated summer house within earshot is harder to satisfy than a village.

**Why.** Consultants routinely model to the 40 dB(A) residential night figure and miss this; the redesign when it surfaces is structural, not cosmetic.

**Here.** Icelandic summer houses are scattered without settlement pattern. Survey every structure within 1.5 km and establish its legal use class, not its appearance.

#### How a stage actually complies at the boundary

A 30 m stage's boundary noise is not the stage — it is plant. Chillers, standby generators, dust extraction, compressors, air handling and the ventilation of a 6,000 m2 room. Target total plant at ≤35 dB(A) at the nearest dwelling so shooting activity retains 5 dB of headroom under the 40 dB(A) night limit.

**Why.** Compliance is designed into the plant yard, the acoustic bund and the attenuator selection, not argued at the boundary after handover.

#### Construction noise hours and the exemption route

Reglugerð 724/2008 Tafla IV: general construction weekdays 07:00–21:00, weekends and public holidays 10:00–19:00. Especially loud work (piling, breaking, rock) weekdays 07:00–19:00 only and not at weekends. Undanþága (exemption) is from the heilbrigðisnefnd. Enforcement sits with heilbrigðisnefndir (art. 11).

**Why.** Piling on a Saturday is the fastest way to turn a neutral neighbour into an objector for the operating life of the lot.

#### Filming light controlled by activity, not by fixture

Shooting light cannot meet a fixture standard: 18 kW HMIs, balloon lights, moonlight rigs, and a 2,400 m2 tank lit from above have no cut-off. Control by activity instead — pre-notified night-exterior nights, capped per month, with modelled sky-glow contours issued to neighbours in advance.

**Why.** Pretending filming light can meet a cut-off condition is dishonest and gets caught the first night the backlot is lit.

#### The June/December daylight inversion as a consent parameter

At 64.2°N there is no astronomical darkness from roughly mid-May to late July and civil twilight barely ends at the solstice; December gives roughly 4–5 hours of usable daylight (estimate — verify against Almanak Háskóla Íslands). Night exteriors are a winter product; day exteriors are a summer product.

**Why.** A backlot condition written without seasons forces night shooting into precisely the months when no darkness physically exists.

**Here.** The inverse is the pitch: December offers roughly 19 hours of shootable night exterior, which almost no competing lot on earth can sell.

#### Fornleifaskráning before the plan, not before the permit

Lög um menningarminjar 80/2012 art. 16: archaeological survey must be completed before the aðalskipulag and before the deiliskipulag are finalised, and the body responsible for the plan bears the cost (art. 16(2)). Commission a full 809 ha walkover plus desk study in year one.

**Why.** The survey is a precondition of plan-making, not a condition on a permit. Commissioned late, it stops the plan dead.

#### The 100-year rule and the protection radii

80/2012 art. 3: any human-made remains 100 years or older are automatically protected — ruins, field boundaries, sheep folds, tracks, charcoal pits, boundary cairns, shielings, graves. Art. 22: 100 m protection zone around friðlýstar (scheduled) remains; 15 m around other protected remains.

**Why.** An invisible turf sheepfold sterilises a 15 m radius; a single scheduled site sterilises 3.1 hectares of whatever you had planned there.

**Here.** 809 ha of family farmland will carry dozens of features. Estimate 30–80 recorded sites, and design the lot around them rather than through them.

#### The archaeological weight of this specific valley

The Mosfell Archaeological Project (Jesse Byock, UCLA, with UCSB; seven field seasons) excavated at Hrísbrú in Mosfellsdalur a Viking-age longhouse, an early Christian church and graveyard, and a ship-prow-shaped cremation feature — the first archaeological evidence of cremation found in Iceland.

**Why.** This valley is internationally published saga-age archaeology. Any find here is news, and news is an objection with a global mailing list.

**Here.** Mosfellskirkja, Gljúfrasteinn (Halldór Laxness's house, now a national museum) and Hrísbrú all sit in the same valley. Treat the valley's cultural standing as a design input, not a risk register line.

#### Chance-find protocol rehearsed before it is needed

80/2012 art. 24: on discovery, work must stop immediately (stöðva framkvæmd án tafar) and Minjastofnun Íslands assesses promptly and decides whether and how work resumes. Art. 36(2): a Minjastofnun permit is required for any archaeological investigation involving ground disturbance.

**Why.** An unrehearsed stop-work on a 200-person site costs more per day than a standing archaeological watching brief costs per month.

#### Nature Conservation Act article 61 habitats and features

Náttúruverndarlög 60/2013 art. 61 specially protects wetlands (mýrar, flóar and water bodies) ≥20,000 m2, ponds and lakes ≥1,000 m2, distinctive or ecologically important birch woodland and remnants containing old trees, post-glacial lava fields, craters and tuff formations, waterfalls and their immediate surroundings, and hot springs.

**Why.** Two hectares of wet ground on an 809 ha site is not spare land — it is a statutorily protected habitat that cannot simply be filled.

#### The article 61 procedure and the reasoned-departure risk

60/2013 art. 61: before damaging a protected feature the developer needs a framkvæmdaleyfi or byggingarleyfi and the municipality must obtain opinions from Náttúruverndarstofnun, Náttúrufræðistofnun and the local nature-conservation committee, must give special reasons in writing if it decides against them, and must impose damage-minimising conditions.

**Why.** Going against a statutory nature opinion is lawful but must be reasoned in writing — which produces exactly the document an appellant needs.

#### Breeding-bird survey and a clearance season

Survey the site for breeding waders — golden plover, whimbrel, snipe, oystercatcher, redshank — plus greylag, and check any pond for red-throated diver. Bind a vegetation-clearance and topsoil-strip season into the construction conditions; estimate no strip between 1 May and 31 July.

**Why.** Iceland holds internationally significant shares of several breeding wader populations. A wader objection is well-evidenced and very hard to rebut.

**Here.** The bird season and the only reliable construction weather window are the same months. Plan the programme knowing that, or lose a year discovering it.

#### Operating hours split between inside and outside

Seek unrestricted 24/7 hours inside sealed stages and support buildings — the building is the mitigation and the acoustic case proves it — with a separate, tighter regime for backlot, tank, exterior lighting, HGV movements and helicopter operations. Never accept one hours condition covering the whole site.

**Why.** A single blanket hours condition applied to a soundstage destroys the product. Separated, the stage case is straightforward to win.

#### Construction environmental management plan as the enforced document

A CEMP secured by condition: haul routes, wheel washing, dust suppression and monitoring, working hours, noise and vibration limits with monitoring positions, pollution prevention, bunded refuelling, waste and materials tracking, ecology and archaeology protocols, and a named site liaison officer.

**Why.** It is the document the municipality enforces daily for three years. Write it, or accept one written by somebody who has never built anything.

#### Seismic and volcanic design carried through the EIA and permit

Design to Eurocode 8 with the Icelandic National Annex (verify agR for the capital area). The South Iceland Seismic Zone produced M6.5 and M6.6 events in 2000 and M6.3 at Hveragerði in 2008. Ash loading, ash ingress and gas contingency belong in the EIA and the building-permit case.

**Why.** Ash is simultaneously a structural load, an HVAC filtration case and a business-continuity case. Regulators will ask; so will insurers and neighbours.

**Here.** Reykjanes has erupted repeatedly since 2021, with an M5.3 and the evacuation of Grindavík in November 2023. This is an operational plan, not a paragraph.

#### Weather written into the programme the conditions attach to

Reykjavík 1991–2020: 875.9 mm precipitation across 154.9 days ≥1.0 mm, 101.8 cm snowfall across 55.1 snow days, monthly means 0.7 °C (January) to 11.6 °C (July). Lowland wind reaches 18 m/s on 10–20 days a year; the Icelandic records are 62.5 m/s sustained and 74.2 m/s gust.

**Why.** Crane-down days, cladding windows and weather float must sit in the programme that phasing conditions are written against, or every date slips.

#### Fire and rescue access set at campus scale

Agree with Slökkvilið höfuðborgarsvæðisins: access road widths and turning circles for the largest appliance, hydrant spacing and flow rates, a site water store sized for the largest stage, a whole-site fire plan and a helicopter landing point. The 2,400 m2 tank can serve as a static water supply — offer it.

**Why.** The fire authority's access requirements set road geometry across the entire lot, and road geometry sets the deiliskipulag layout.

**Here.** Response times from Reykjavík are 15 minutes in good conditions. In a winter storm they are not, which is the argument for on-site suppression capacity.

#### Crew housing consented as housing, not smuggled onto an industrial designation

Housing imported heads of department needs its own land-use designation — íbúðarbyggð or a defined dvalarstaður — with its own deiliskipulag, services and objections. Trilith built the Town at Trilith beside a 700-acre studio site with a 400-acre backlot and 34 stages rather than pretend the need away.

**Why.** Sleeping crew in unconsented accommodation on an industrial designation is the enforcement action that closes a lot.

#### The rebate sunset as the fixed point of the programme

Lög 43/1999: 25% base reimbursement; 35% where Icelandic spend is ≥ ISK 350 million, with ≥30 work days including ≥10 shooting days in Iceland and ≥50 people taxed in Iceland working ≥50 work days. Application to the committee before production starts. The scheme expires 31 December 2028 (art. 9).

**Why.** A consent programme landing after the sunset has a different business case entirely. The date is a legal fact, not a planning assumption.

**Here.** Plan the consent backwards from 31 December 2028 and lobby for extension in parallel. The planning programme and the rebate-extension campaign are the same project.

#### Grid connection resolved before the plan fixes the load

Confirm capacity in writing with Landsnet (transmission) and Veitur (distribution) before the deiliskipulag fixes electrical load. The premise that Landsvirkjun has refused new industrial supply could not be verified here and must be established in writing, because the answer decides whether on-site generation enters the EIA.

**Why.** On-site generation changes the noise case, the air-quality case, the fuel-storage starfsleyfi and the carbon chapter simultaneously.

**Here.** Geothermal district heat is separately supplied and usually not the constraint; electricity is. Keep heat and power as two distinct applications with two distinct answers.

#### Appeals exposure to úrskurðarnefnd umhverfis- og auðlindamála

Lög 130/2011 art. 4: appeal within one month of the decision or its publication. Environmental and outdoor-recreation NGOs with ≥30 members, open membership, published annual reports and audited accounts have standing. Art. 5: an appeal does not automatically suspend the permit, but the board may order work stopped.

**Why.** One month is the window in which a year's consent can be put at risk by a thirty-member society founded specifically for the purpose.

### Expected

#### Pre-application with skipulagsfulltrúi and skipulagsnefnd

Mosfellsbær's planning officer and planning committee. Sveitarstjórnarlög 138/2011 arts. 15–16: agendas and minutes are published and meetings are public unless closed by resolution. Estimate: 6–9 months of pre-application engagement before the lýsing is lodged.

**Why.** Everything a small municipality's committee sees becomes public. Surprising them in public is the fastest way to lose them permanently.

#### Councillor conflict-of-interest mapping before the first vote

Sveitarstjórnarlög 138/2011 art. 20: a councillor must recuse where they or close kin have particular and substantial interests exceeding others'. Self-reporting is mandatory; the council rules on disputes. Map every councillor's land, family and business ties to the site and minute it.

**Why.** A decision carried by a disqualified councillor is voidable — after the money is spent.

**Here.** Mosfellsdalur has 245 residents (2026). On family land, kinship to the applicant is itself the disqualifying interest, and everyone already knows who is related to whom.

#### Local referendum (íbúakosning) exposure

Sveitarstjórnarlög 138/2011 provides for binding resident referendums on council decisions on petition of a defined share of the electorate (verify the current threshold and its scope — the Act also carries population-specific provisions). Mosfellsbær has roughly 10,000 voters.

**Why.** A referendum can overturn a consent the council already granted, at the point where capital is irrevocably committed.

#### Cumulative and in-combination assessment

Assess the studio alongside other consented and planned development in Mosfellsbær and the capital region — housing growth areas, road schemes, and any other large electrical load seeking the same grid capacity. Estimate: traffic and landscape are where cumulative objections concentrate.

**Why.** Objectors win on cumulative traffic and cumulative landscape change far more often than on a single project's own numbers.

#### Numbered comment-and-response register, published

Both 111/2021 and 123/2010 require publication of representations and a reasoned response to each. Run a numbered register from the lýsing onward and publish it at each stage. Estimate 200–800 representations for a project of this profile.

**Why.** An unanswered representation is a procedural defect. A published, answered one is a defence at the appeals board.

#### Aviation obstruction lighting reconciled with the dark-sky condition

A 30 m stage, cranes above 60 m and any mast may require aviation obstruction lighting and consultation with Isavia / Samgöngustofa (verify height thresholds and the safeguarding surfaces for Reykjavík Airport). Specify infrared or night-dimmed red where permitted.

**Why.** Two regulators will impose contradictory lighting conditions if they are allowed to write them independently of each other.

#### Standing watching brief and an escrowed rescue budget

Retain a licensed archaeologist on watching brief for all topsoil strip, at pre-agreed rates, with an escrowed rescue-excavation provision. Estimate ISK 40–120 million for a site this size; a single longhouse excavation can consume an entire field season.

**Why.** The budget line exists whether it is planned or not. Planned, it does not stop the construction programme.

**Here.** Excavation seasons are short. A find in September may not be resolvable until the following June — build that into the float, not the contingency.

#### Náttúruminjaskrá and hverfisvernd check

Check all three parts of the Náttúruminjaskrá (A: protected areas, B: five-year implementation list, C: candidate sites) across and adjacent to the site, plus any hverfisvernd (municipal conservation designation) in the aðalskipulag, including the Leirvogur estuary and the Varmá and Kaldakvísl corridors.

**Why.** A C-part candidate site carries no prohibition but carries enormous argumentative weight at a hearing and with the appeals board.

#### Vehicle movement conditions and a negotiated wrap-out allowance

Pre-empt an HGV condition: estimate 07:00–19:00 weekdays, none before 08:00 Saturdays, none Sundays, on a defined route with a movement log. Negotiate an explicit allowance for load-in and wrap-out nights — estimate 12 per year with 48 hours' notice to the municipality and named neighbours.

**Why.** Wrap-out is inherently nocturnal. An allowance negotiated up front costs nothing; an exemption sought later costs a production its dates.

#### Phasing conditions with mitigation front-loaded

Expect conditions tying occupation of each phase to delivery of infrastructure — junction works, attenuation, landscape bunds, planting. Agree a phasing plan that delivers the mitigation neighbours can see in phase one and defers what they cannot see.

**Why.** Mitigation delivered first buys tolerance for three more years of construction. Delivered last, it buys nothing at all.

#### Borrow pits, cut-and-fill balance and a second consent

Winning fill from within the 809 ha needs its own framkvæmdaleyfi under reglugerð 772/2012 art. 5, with an extraction plan showing the landform during and after working (art. 7); quarrying above threshold is itself an EIA project. Importing aggregate instead means thousands of additional HGV movements.

**Why.** The cut-and-fill balance decides both the traffic case and whether a second, separately appealable consent process is required.

#### What the lot pays the municipality, published

Fasteignaskattur (property tax) on commercial property is set by the municipality within statutory bands — verify the current Mosfellsbær commercial rate (typically around 1.32–1.65% of assessed value). Publish the annual figure. Estimate: a lot this size becomes one of the municipality's largest single ratepayers.

**Why.** The strongest community argument is arithmetic the council can enter into its own budget, not a promise about jobs.

#### Appeal timescale carried as a funded programme risk

The board normally rules within three months of receiving the file, six months for complex matters (130/2011 art. 4), with expedition where substantial financial interests are at stake. Estimate 6–9 months of real elapsed time; carry it as a named risk with a funded standstill position.

**Why.** Starting work under appeal is lawful and sometimes right, but only if the cost of unwinding is known before the first digger arrives.

#### Conditions register run as a workstream

A consent of this scale arrives with an estimated 40–90 conditions. Run a register recording owner, evidence required, discharge authority, lead time and which trigger it blocks — pre-commencement, pre-superstructure, pre-occupation. Review fortnightly and audit before each phase.

**Why.** Unnoticed pre-commencement conditions are the single most common cause of unlawful development on large sites.

#### What modern lots do that old ones do not

Shinfield Studios (Wokingham Borough Council; permanent stages approved unanimously 13 October 2021; 18 stages totalling 433,649 sq ft, two at 41,000 sq ft and 35 m; 9-acre backlot; 38 workshops; ~£250m) consented on a science-park designation with a university landlord. Trilith consented land use and its own town together.

**Why.** Old lots grew by accretion under no plan. New ones are consented as one masterplan with a community deal attached, and are judged forever against that deal.

**Here.** Sugar Land has what neither had: a single family owner, a single title, and no pre-existing neighbours on the land itself. The consent should be drawn once, wide, and for thirty years.

### State of the art

#### Travel plan, contracted shuttle and a parking cap

Bind a travel plan into the deiliskipulag: contracted coaches from Reykjavík (15 minutes), park-and-ride, EV charging, and a parking cap rather than a minimum. Estimate 1 space per 2.5 on-site staff plus segregated unit-vehicle and truck compounds.

**Why.** Unrestrained parking is what makes a rural road fail. A capped, shuttled lot is what makes the traffic case winnable.

**Here.** Winter driving and near-zero December daylight make a professional shuttle a safety measure as much as a planning one.

#### A written backlot activity code in the consent

Exteriors breach limits, not stages: crowd, vehicle action, gunfire, pyrotechnics, helicopters, drones, rain towers, wind machines, unit-base generators and tank wave plant. Write an activity code into the deiliskipulag with categories, per-event decibel budgets, annual caps and neighbour notice periods.

**Why.** A consent that silently forbids the things a backlot exists for is worse than having no backlot at all.

#### Permanent boundary noise monitoring with a public dashboard

Three to five fixed Class 1 monitors (IEC 61672-1) at the nearest receptors, logging LAeq, LAFmax and one-third-octave data continuously, with a public web dashboard and 24-month data retention. Estimate ISK 12–25 million capital, ISK 4–8 million per year running.

**Why.** When a complaint arrives at 02:00 the only useful answer is a timestamped measurement. Without one you concede by default.

**Here.** Modern lots increasingly carry monitoring as a condition rather than a courtesy; verify the individual condition text at Shinfield and Sunset Waltham Cross before drafting yours.

#### Complaint handling written into the consent

A named duty phone answered 24/7 by someone with authority to stop an activity, a logged complaint register, response-time targets (estimate: acknowledge within 1 hour, substantive answer within 24 hours), and quarterly publication to the municipality.

**Why.** Regulators judge a lot on complaint handling far more than on decibels. A good log survives a bad night; a bad log loses a good one.

#### A voluntary obtrusive-light standard adopted as a condition

There appears to be no Icelandic dark-sky statute (verify). Adopt CIE 150 / ILP GN01 environmental zone E1–E2 voluntarily in the deiliskipulag: 0% upward light ratio from luminaires, capped vertical illuminance at neighbouring windows pre- and post-curfew, and a 23:00 curfew. Verify exact zone figures before committing.

**Why.** Volunteering a hard lighting standard is the cheapest goodwill available and forecloses an objection that is otherwise unanswerable.

#### Permanent fixture specification

Full cut-off, DarkSky-Approved or equivalent, ≤2700 K correlated colour temperature for all permanent site, road and car-park lighting. Motion-triggered where security permits, site-wide dimming and curfew schedule, amber LED on the perimeter, and no uplighting of any building at any time.

**Why.** Colour temperature above 3000 K is what makes a lot visible from the next valley and what destroys aurora viewing for everyone around it.

**Here.** Aurora visibility is a regional economic asset. A lot that dims on the aurora forecast buys more community credit than any donation.

#### Wetland mapping and a binding restoration offer

Map every wetland ≥2 ha and every pond ≥1,000 m2 before the plan is drawn. Where drainage is unavoidable, offer restoration elsewhere on the 809 ha at a stated ratio (estimate 2:1 by area) written into the deiliskipulag as a binding obligation with a monitoring period.

**Why.** Iceland has drained much of its lowland wetland. Restoring some is the most credible mitigation available and reads as genuine rather than transactional.

#### Public access rights across the land (almannaréttur)

Icelandic law gives the public rights of passage over uncultivated land. A fenced, gated 809 ha lot removes access exercised for generations. Map desire lines, then negotiate and dedicate permanent perimeter and through routes in the deiliskipulag rather than defending a fence.

**Why.** Fencing land people have always walked is the grievance that outlives every other one, and it is entirely avoidable at the drawing stage.

**Here.** Esja, Helgafell and the Mosfellsdalur paths are heavily walked by capital-region residents. This is not an abstract right here.

#### Standing community liaison group with the actual stakeholders

A liaison group with the municipality, Mosfellsdalur residents, the angling association, hiking clubs, the church and Gljúfrasteinn — monthly through construction, quarterly after. Published minutes. A named liaison officer who lives locally and answers the phone.

**Why.** In a municipality of 13,715 the objectors and the future workforce are the same people. This is a relationship, not a consultation exercise.

#### Training pipeline as the answer to the crew-base objection

Iceland's working crew base is roughly 1,000 people nationally. Fund apprenticeships and a stage-technician programme with a Reykjavík college, and write local-hire commitments into the agreement with the municipality alongside the consent.

**Why.** Importing and housing every head of department is highly visible. Training locals is the only credible answer to "this is not for us".

**Here.** With a national base of about a thousand, four stages running simultaneously would absorb a large share of it. The training case is also a capacity case.

### What gets got wrong

- The brief says "a municipality of a few thousand people". Mosfellsbær had 13,715 residents in 2025 across 186 km2 (Wikipedia). Mosfellsdalur itself had 245 (2026). The community relations problem is therefore two-layered: a valley of 245 who will live with the lot, and a town of 13,700 whose council votes on it. 809 ha is 4.3% of the entire municipality's land area.
- The EIA is almost certainly mandatory, not screened. Lög 111/2021 Viðauki 1 item 10.01 puts industrial development at ≥20,000 m2 of site or building floor area into flokkur A, where assessment is automatic. 13,200 m2 of stage plus support will exceed that. Budget 24-36 months (estimate) from matsáætlun to final agency opinion.
- The tightest noise number on the lot is 35 dB(A) for frístundabyggð (summer-house areas) under reglugerð 724/2008 - 5 dB below the 40 dB(A) night limit at permanent dwellings, and applying day and night alike. A single lawfully designated summer house within earshot is a harder constraint than a village. Most acoustic models are run against the residential figure and miss it.
- Archaeological survey (fornleifaskráning) is a precondition of plan-making, not a condition on a permit: lög 80/2012 art. 16 requires it before both the aðalskipulag and the deiliskipulag are finalised, and the party responsible for the plan pays. Anything 100 years old is automatically protected (art. 3), with 15 m buffers around protected remains and 100 m around scheduled ones (art. 22).
- Mosfellsdalur is internationally published Viking-age archaeology. The Mosfell Archaeological Project (Jesse Byock, UCLA, seven field seasons) excavated a longhouse, an early Christian church and graveyard, and a ship-prow cremation feature at Hrísbrú - the first cremation evidence found in Iceland. Gljúfrasteinn, Halldór Laxness's house and a national museum, is in the same valley.
- Agency identities have changed and submissions will go astray. skipulag.is now redirects to island.is and the planning-agency page is branded Húsnæðis-, mannvirkja- og skipulagsstofnun (HMS); the text of lög 7/1998 now names Umhverfis- og orkustofnun, and lög 60/2013 names Náttúruverndarstofnun. Verify every statutory addressee and vesting date before filing anything.
- The appeal window is one month and NGO standing costs almost nothing: úrskurðarnefnd umhverfis- og auðlindamála takes appeals from any environmental or outdoor-recreation organisation with 30 or more members, open membership and published accounts (lög 130/2011 art. 4). An appeal does not automatically suspend the permit, but the board can order work stopped.
- The capital-region regional plan (Svæðisskipulag höfuðborgarsvæðisins 2015-2040) can veto a municipality that has already said yes. I could not verify its current growth-boundary (vaxtarmörk) text from here. Test it before land is committed - it is the likeliest single hard stop on the project.
- Vegagerðin, not the municipality, controls the junction. Vegalög 80/2007 art. 29 requires its authority to connect to a þjóðvegur; art. 32 sets building setbacks of ≥30 m from centreline for stofnvegir, ≥15 m for other national roads and 40 m at junctions, extendable to 150 m. Reglugerð 180/2015 governs junction spacing and design.
- The rebate is the clock. Lög 43/1999 gives 25%, or 35% where Icelandic spend is ≥ ISK 350 million with ≥30 work days including ≥10 shooting days and ≥50 people taxed in Iceland - and the scheme expires 31 December 2028 (art. 9). The planning programme must be drawn backwards from that date, with an extension campaign run in parallel.
- There is no Icelandic dark-sky statute that I could find (verify). That means light control is whatever is volunteered into the deiliskipulag - which is an opportunity, not a gap. Adopting CIE 150 / ILP GN01 zone E1-E2 and ≤2700 K fixtures voluntarily forecloses an objection that cannot otherwise be answered.
- Filming light cannot be made to meet a fixture standard. 18 kW HMIs, balloon lights and a lit 2,400 m2 tank have no cut-off. Control night exteriors by pre-notified, capped activity with published sky-glow contours, and say so honestly in the EIA rather than being caught the first night the backlot is lit.
- The breeding-bird season and the only reliable construction weather window are the same months. A clearance embargo (estimate: 1 May to 31 July) has to be in the programme from day one, not discovered in year two.
- This session's WebSearch budget was exhausted before I began, so every fact above was obtained by direct WebFetch of primary sources - althingi.is statute texts, island.is regulation texts, and Wikipedia for non-legal facts. Sites I could not reach: mos.is (403, so Mosfellsbær's current aðalskipulag years are unverified), skipulag.is (redirected), theilp.org.uk and darksky.org (403, so the GN01 zone tables and DarkSky fixture thresholds above are from general knowledge and need verification), Vegagerðin traffic counts, Landsvirkjun's capacity statements, and the Broxbourne and Wokingham condition schedules.
- Things marked estimate that must be replaced with real figures before anything is filed: trip generation and peak-hour flows, the tank volume and water balance, plant noise targets, the archaeology provision, the number of conditions, phase durations, the fasteignaskattur rate, the Eurocode 8 Icelandic National Annex agR, and December daylight hours from Almanak Háskóla Íslands.

---

## 11. Amenity, hospitality and why people choose you

*campus scale, Sugar Land Studios, Mosfellsbær*

### Essential

#### Commissary — the main dining hall

1,150 m² hall; 420 seated covers plus 120 in a glazed wind-shelter; 3 hot lines, deli, grab-and-go; peak 1,400 covers in a 90-min push; 400 m² kitchen (1:2.9 back-of-house to front); 05:30–22:00. Sized at 1.25x a peak on-lot headcount of ~1,100. Estimate.

**Why.** Without it crew drive off-lot to eat; a 25-min round trip to Mosfellsbær costs an hour of the shooting day and breaks meal-penalty timing.

**Here.** In a 20 m/s winter gale nobody leaves for a sandwich. Nearest alternative food is Mosfellsbær centre, roughly 5 km. The commissary is the only realistic lunch, which raises the bar on it — it is not a fallback, it is the whole offer.

#### Allergen, dietary, cultural and food-safety regime

All 14 EU-declarable allergens labelled at point of service; ≥2 vegan and ≥1 gluten-free hot option every service; halal and kosher on 24 h notice; separate fryer and board discipline; HACCP plan audited by the regional Heilbrigðiseftirlit, which also licenses the premises.

**Why.** One anaphylaxis event on a site 25 minutes from a hospital is a fatality risk and a production shutdown, not a customer-service complaint.

**Here.** Food-premises licensing is regional public health (Heilbrigðiseftirlit), not national. Build the approval into the programme, not the opening week.

#### Drying rooms, boot rooms and lockers

4 heated drying rooms, one per stage cluster, 60 garment positions each, 35 °C and 12 air changes/hour on a geothermal coil; 900 lockers; boot wash and grit trap at every crew entrance.

**Why.** Gear that never dries makes crew cold, sick and slow, and destroys radios, batteries and costume. Cheapest morale spend on a wet site.

**Here.** Southwest Iceland is persistently wet, and lowland winds reach 18 m/s on 10–20 days a year (Wikipedia, Climate of Iceland), driving rain horizontally into every door.

#### Occupational health clinic and on-site medical cover

Staffed whenever a unit shoots: minimum 1 paramedic, nurse-led clinic 3 days/week, 2 treatment bays, est. 14 AEDs (one per 150 m), oxygen, spinal board, hypothermia kit. Water-stage days add a dive medic. National emergency number is 112.

**Why.** The nearest hospital is Landspítali in Reykjavík. Blue-light response to a rural site is not a five-minute proposition; first response is yours.

**Here.** Helicopter EMS belongs to the Coast Guard (Landhelgisgæslan), is shared with search and rescue, and can be committed elsewhere or grounded outright by wind.

#### Meeting rooms and boardroom

12 bookable rooms (4/6/10/20 person) plus a 30-seat boardroom with a 5 m table, dual 98-inch displays and certified video conferencing. One hard-wired room with no wireless at all for deal and legal sessions.

**Why.** Production is meetings. If crew take studio calls in corridors you get noise, leaks and no record of what was agreed.

**Here.** Iceland runs UTC year-round with no daylight saving, so Los Angeles is 7–8 hours behind and the reliable overlap window is roughly 16:00–19:00 local.

#### Accessibility across the whole amenity estate

Step-free from every car park to every amenity; hearing loops in theatre, auditorium and reception; 2 wheelchair positions per screening room; accessible WC and shower per building; high-contrast bilingual signage; snow-melt on all accessible routes.

**Why.** Accessibility fails first in snow and wind: a ramp under 30 cm of drift is not a ramp. Design the clearing plan, not just the gradient.

**Here.** Icelandic building regulations set the physical requirements; the operational commitment is keeping those specific routes clear and de-iced at 05:00 in January.

#### Covered links between every building

Every amenity building reachable from every stage under cover: 3–4 m wide colonnades, wind-baffled at both ends, lit to 100 lux, gritted and snow-melted. Target: nobody walks more than 30 m in the open between stage, commissary and welfare.

**Why.** A 200 m walk in horizontal sleet means people skip lunch, skip the gym and eat at their station. The link is what makes every other amenity real.

**Here.** This is the highest-value money on the whole lot and the thing Georgia, California and even Hertfordshire lots never have to build. Omit it and the amenity budget is wasted.

#### Parking, lit and ploughed

Est. 700 spaces; lighting to 20 lux on wind-rated columns; snow-clearance SLA with a 05:00 deadline; 60 EV bays; 40 sheltered priority bays for early calls and accessible users; 100 engine block-heater sockets; 60-space visitor court outside the secure line.

**Why.** Parking is the first and last experience of the lot every day. Unlit, unploughed, exposed parking is the complaint that never stops.

**Here.** Nobody will clear your car park at 04:00 on a January Sunday unless it is a contracted commitment with a named deadline and a penalty.

#### HOD long-stay apartments

24 serviced apartments of 55–90 m², two and three bed, full kitchen, washer-dryer, storage, parking, pet-tolerant, let for 12–30 weeks, fibre at 500 Mbps symmetric or better. Furnished to a standard a department head accepts sight-unseen.

**Why.** The premise is that heads of department are imported and housed. Housing is a line in every HOD deal and its quality decides whether they say yes.

**Here.** The capital-region rental market is tight and short-term letting is regulated. No production can assemble twenty family flats at eight weeks' notice.

#### Connectivity as an amenity

Guest wifi on its own VLAN, one-click access, 200 Mbps per user minimum, full coverage including courtyards, drying rooms and the lodge; plus in-building cellular repeaters for all three Icelandic networks (Síminn, Vodafone, Nova).

**Why.** Crew judge connectivity before they judge the coffee. Dead spots on an 809 ha site generate more complaints than anything else on this list.

**Here.** Mosfellsdalur terrain shadows cell coverage. Repeaters are not optional, and a production's ability to move dailies depends on the backhaul behind them.

#### Amenity as a published contract term

Publish what the stage rate includes (gym, pool, shuttle, parking, wifi, welfare, quiet rooms, physio triage, drying rooms) and what is metered (meals per head, office, suites, lodge keys, event space). It belongs on the rate card, not in the wrap invoice.

**Why.** Productions do not distrust price, they distrust surprise. A lot that itemises hospitality cleanly is the lot that gets rebooked.

**Here.** Costs deductible under Iceland's Income Tax Act and incurred in Iceland are reimbursement-eligible (filminiceland.com), so lot-supplied catering, housing and transport can flow into the claim.

#### Structuring bookings toward the 35% rate

Model every bid against the enhanced test: at least ISK 350m of Icelandic cost, at least 30 working days in Iceland with 10 or more shooting days, and 50+ crew for 50+ working days on Icelandic-taxed salaries (filminiceland.com). Baseline is 25%, no minimum, no cap.

**Why.** Ten points on an ISK 2bn Icelandic spend is ISK 200m. No amenity you can build is worth as much as helping a production clear that threshold.

**Here.** The scheme is legislated only to 31 December 2028 (filminiceland.com). Every pitch made now is also a bet on renewal, and amenity is what survives the cliff.

#### The argument itself: lots now compete on hospitality

Twenty years ago a lot sold cubic metres, power and a gate. Trilith now sells a town: 34 stages beside a K-12 school, a nine-screen cinema, an 1,800-seat auditorium and a Marriott hotel (Wikipedia). Shinfield sells 'The Hub' café. Sunset sells concierge support and bike storage.

**Why.** 13,200 m² of column-free volume is buyable in six countries. Hospitality is the only part of the offer that cannot be replicated by a competitor's steel.

**Here.** At this distance and in this weather hospitality is not a differentiator, it is the entry ticket. Nobody picks a lot in a gale 12 km from a 140,000-person city for the stage alone.

### Expected

#### Second kitchen for unit catering, physically separate

280 m² cook-chill kitchen with its own dock and shutter, 2 blast chillers (90 kg to +3 °C in 90 min), 6 hot-hold trolleys, surge to 2,000 covers. Let to a unit caterer under concession licence, not run in-house. Estimate.

**Why.** Unit catering runs to the shooting day, not the lot's day. One shared kitchen means the commissary dies whenever a unit feeds 600 at 03:00.

**Here.** Iceland has very few large unit caterers. The room has to be good enough that a London or Budapest caterer flies a head chef in and works from day one without shipping a kitchen.

#### Coffee house run as a concession

220 m² speciality coffee bar operated by a third party, not by catering: 2 three-group machines plus batch brew, 2 baristas at peak, 05:00–19:00, est. 150–190 kg roasted beans per week at full lot occupancy.

**Why.** Coffee is what crew judge you on in the first ten minutes. Filter from an urn signals every other corner you have cut.

**Here.** Reykjavík has a serious speciality scene (Reykjavík Roasters, Kaffibrennslan, Te & Kaffi). Anything less on the lot reads as provincial to Icelandic crew and they will drive to town for it.

#### Stage-cluster kiosks and 24-hour provisioning

One 25–40 m² barista/soup/pastry kiosk per stage cluster (4 total), plus 24 h hot-drink and vending walls in both office buildings and the workshop block. Kiosks open 05:00, close 30 min after last unit wrap. Estimate.

**Why.** A 6,000 m² stage is a 200 m walk from the commissary. Without kiosks crew build kettle corners at their station and you lose the social centre.

**Here.** June nights and December 03:00 calls mean lunch lands at any hour. A fixed 12:00–14:00 service window fails for half the year at this latitude.

#### Night canteen — hot food at any hour

Hot service within 20 min, 24/7, whenever a unit is on the lot: 90 m² night kitchen inside the commissary shell with its own shutter, 1 chef plus 1 assistant on nights, 120-cover capacity. Estimate.

**Why.** Split days and night exteriors are the norm at this latitude. Cold sandwiches at 04:00 in February is how a lot earns its reputation.

**Here.** Reykjavík sees under five hours of daylight in midwinter (Wikipedia, Reykjavík). Exterior work migrates into darkness and the catering day inverts with it.

#### Meal pricing and per-head account billing

Fixed published crew price (est. ISK 2,200–2,800, about EUR 15–19, for a hot main with salad and drink) against a true cost near ISK 3,400. Bill productions on a per-head account, never cash at the till.

**Why.** Cash tills create queues, tips and a decision at every lunch. Account billing makes eating frictionless and makes the spend visible in the deal.

**Here.** Icelandic food inputs sit well above the EU average because almost everything is imported by sea, so the subsidy is real money, not a rounding line.

#### Water strategy — no bottled water on site

Zero bottled water anywhere on the lot. Est. 30 chilled still/sparkling refill stations, one per ~40 occupants, plus 250 issued steel bottles per production. Capital-region cold tap is untreated, unchlorinated groundwater.

**Why.** Bottled water is the most visible waste stream on a production and the easiest sustainability win to put in writing in a bid.

**Here.** The hot tap is geothermal and smells of sulphur. Tell visiting crew on day one or field the same complaint for a fortnight.

#### Private dining room and chef's table

One 40 m² private room, 14 covers, own service door, blackout, AV, direct link to the executive suites; plus a 24-cover room convertible to a tasting and demo kitchen. Bookable by the hour.

**Why.** Deals, difficult conversations and talent who cannot sit in a canteen all need a room. Without one they happen off-lot and you lose the day.

**Here.** Reykjavík's fine-dining count is small and books months ahead. The on-lot room is often the only discreet option inside 30 minutes.

#### Gym, free at the point of use

550–650 m², badge access 24/7, 12 cardio stations, full rack and free-weight floor, a functional turf strip for stunt and rigging conditioning, and 2 studios (one 90 m² for classes and rehab). Free to any live lot pass.

**Why.** Trilith gives complimentary Wellness Center access to all crew filming at the studio (trilithstudios.com). Charge for it and usage collapses along with the selling point.

**Here.** Icelanders treat a gym and a pool as baseline municipal infrastructure. Mosfellsbær's Lágafellslaug is 5 km away and shuts before a late wrap.

#### Lactation rooms

3 dedicated rooms, 8 m² or larger, one per occupied building: lockable, sink, fridge, comfortable chair, power. Never a converted toilet, never reassigned to another use, bookable but always available.

**Why.** A returning parent with nowhere to pump does not come back. This is a retention line, not a compliance box.

**Here.** Iceland's shared parental leave is among the longest in Europe, so crew return to work mid-shoot far more often than US or UK scheduling assumes.

#### Blackout strategy for the midnight sun

Every bed, quiet room and rest room blacked out to under 1 lux at 02:00 in June. External solar-control blinds on amenity glazing rated to 40 m/s so they can stay deployed in wind rather than retracting at every gale.

**Why.** Near-continuous daylight wrecks sleep for crew unused to it, and unshaded glazing makes a dayroom unusable for anyone working nights.

**Here.** Daylight is essentially permanent from 20 May to 24 July in the capital region (Wikipedia) — the same window a production wants for long exterior days.

#### Lot shop and pharmacy pickup

150 m² convenience shop: toiletries, thermals, gloves, hats, batteries, cards, tape, OTC medicines, SIM cards, ISK cash-out. Prescription pickup locker with a Mosfellsbær apótek partner. 06:00–20:00. Margin is irrelevant; availability is the point.

**Why.** A crew member who forgot thermals in a 3 °C gale will either buy them on the lot or be useless for the day.

**Here.** There is no 24-hour pharmacy near the site, and imported crew need a working route to repeat prescriptions from arrival, not from week six.

#### Flagship client screening theatre

120 seats, DCI 4K laser, 14 m scope screen, Dolby Atmos 9.1.6 or better, B-chain to ISO 2969, reference 85 dB(C) at -20 dBFS pink, background NC-20, RT60 under 0.4 s, stepped risers, 2 wheelchair positions, 48 cd/m² screen white.

**Why.** A lot without a room a client trusts sends dailies and finals off-site — and that is where the next conversation happens too.

**Here.** Sky Studios Elstree built an on-site screening cinema into a 13-stage campus (Wikipedia). A lot this remote needs one more than a suburban one does, not less.

#### Dailies and review rooms

2 rooms of 20 seats: P3-D65 projection calibrated to 48 cd/m² on a 4 m screen, or a reference mastering display at 1,000 nits for HDR; 5.1; room tone NC-25 or better. 30-min booking blocks, one room permanently reserved for same-day dailies.

**Why.** Colour and performance arguments happen the morning after the shoot. If the only trusted room is the 120-seater, you cannot run two productions at once.

**Here.** Remote sessions with a London or Los Angeles suite are the normal case here, so the room's value depends entirely on the fibre and subsea capacity behind it.

#### Executive and client suites
