6 suites of 45–70 m²: desk, sofa, private WC and shower, kitchenette, secure comms, blackout, own entrance and a buggy route to stage. Serviced daily. Let by the week alongside the stage, not discovered as an extra later.

**Why.** Studio executives and financiers fly in for two days. Where they sit decides whether they believe the production is under control.

**Here.** Visits cluster around flight availability through Keflavík, 50 km away (Wikipedia), so visitors arrive in bursts and need somewhere to work within the hour.

#### VIP arrival and discretion protocol

Screened arrival court with a 4.2 m covered drop, no sightline from any public road, separate pass class, NDA-bound hospitality staff, a contractual no-photography rule, and a route from gate to suite that never crosses the commissary.

**Why.** One phone photograph of a lead in costume is a marketing crisis and the reason a studio quietly never books you again.

**Here.** Iceland has no paparazzi trade but is small and intensely connected. A single supermarket sighting circulates nationally within the hour.

#### Casting suites with their own front of house

3 rooms (one 60 m² with a 4 m clear wall for movement, two 30 m²), a 40-seat waiting lounge with its own WC, and a separate entrance and car park so 200 auditionees never cross the secure line. Self-tape rig, cyc, uplink for remote sessions.

**Why.** Casting is the one activity that brings the public inside. Without a separate front-of-house you either compromise security or send casting to a hotel.

**Here.** With a national crew and performer base near 1,000, extras and open calls reach ordinary Icelanders and volumes are enormous relative to the country's size.

#### Rehearsal, dance and stunt hall

One 500 m² sprung-floor hall, 7 m clear height, mirrored wall with track blackout, tapeable floor, PA, and 3-tonne rated beams for wire rehearsal; plus a 200 m² second hall. Geothermal heated to 19 °C.

**Why.** Rehearsing on a live stage burns a stage day at full rate. Choreography, fight and camera rehearsal need a cheap room that is always free.

**Here.** Outdoor rehearsal is unavailable most of the year. Everything that happens in a Georgia car park has to happen indoors here, and be programmed for.

#### Minors' schoolroom and studio teacher provision

2 schoolrooms of 25 m² with daylight-spectrum lighting, a dedicated minor's rest room and separate WC, capacity 12. Pre-agreed compliance route with Vinnueftirlitið and the local school district for children's working hours.

**Why.** A production with child leads picks the lot that already has the room and the permissions over the one that says it can be arranged.

**Here.** Icelandic rules on children's working hours are strict and enforced by Vinnueftirlitið. Permissions must be arranged in advance, not scrambled during prep.

#### Multi-faith and prayer room

One 25 m² carpeted room with ablution facilities, qibla marked, screenable into two, no imagery, bookable but never double-booked at prayer times. Distinct from the quiet rooms, which serve a different purpose.

**Why.** International crews include practising Muslims, Jews and others. The room costs almost nothing and its absence is noticed immediately.

**Here.** There is very little existing provision outside central Reykjavík, so the lot's room is likely the only one within 15 km of the site.

#### Wind-sheltered outdoor space

Three enclosed courtyards of 400–900 m²: solid wall or dense shelter-belt to the prevailing quadrant, canopies rated to 45 m/s or retracting below 18 m/s, radiant geothermal underfloor to seating terraces, snow-melt to all paths.

**Why.** Outdoor space unusable 250 days a year is decoration. Shelter plus heat is what makes it the place crew actually take a break.

**Here.** Geothermal snow-melt is near-free here and already standard in Reykjavík streets — one of the few places the climate hands something back.

#### Smoking, vaping and snus shelters

4 sheltered, heated, wind-baffled points, 15 m from any door and downwind of every air intake; separate snus benches with bins; butt bins emptied daily; total ban elsewhere on the 809 ha during dry summer windows.

**Why.** Unmanaged smoking beside moss and heath in a dry August is a wildfire ignition source, and near a stage door it is a fire risk.

**Here.** Icelandic nicotine use skews strongly to snus rather than cigarettes, which changes the brief entirely: bins and spitting provision, not just ashtrays.

#### Bikes, e-buggies and EV charging

120 secure covered bike spaces with 40 e-bike charge points and a repair stand; est. 60 lot e-buggies and 20 cargo trikes for inter-building movement; EV charging at 10% of parking (est. 60 bays, 22 kW AC plus 6 DC rapids at 150 kW).

**Why.** Sunset Studios markets on-site parking, EV charging and secured bike storage as headline amenities (sunsetstudios.com). Table stakes now, not green branding.

**Here.** Iceland has one of the highest EV shares of new car sales in the world, so hire cars arriving on the lot will overwhelmingly be electric and will need charge.

#### Reykjavík hotel block agreements

Standing allocations with 3–4 capital-region hotels: est. 80 rooms held to 45 days out at a fixed corporate rate, with a release schedule, guaranteed late check-in, and breakfast service from 04:30 rather than 07:00.

**Why.** Even with a lodge you will overflow. A hotel that will not feed a crew at 04:30 or hold a 01:00 arrival is useless to a production.

**Here.** Keflavík handled 8,159,461 passengers in 2025 (Wikipedia). The tourist peak collides exactly with the shooting season, so allocations must be contracted early.

#### Shuttle to Reykjavík

Fixed runs at call-time minus 75 min inbound and wrap plus 20 min outbound, plus a midday service: 2 x 49-seat coaches and 3 x 16-seat minibuses, 3–4 city pickup points, live tracking in the crew app. Roughly 12 km, 20–30 min.

**Why.** Without a shuttle you get 150 hire cars on the lot and half of them driving tired, in the dark, on ice, after a fourteen-hour day.

**Here.** Strætó serves the capital region at roughly 15-minute peak headways (Strætó bs, Wikipedia), but public service stops long before a 22:00 wrap. The lot must run its own.

#### Keflavík transfer and arrivals concierge

Met-on-arrival for every HOD and client: 50 km on the Route 41 dual carriageway, about 45–50 min (Wikipedia). Flight tracking, fixed vehicles, an ISK card and cash kit, a SIM, a stocked flat and a written weather brief left on the seat.

**Why.** The first ninety minutes in a strange country at 01:00 in a gale set the tone for twenty weeks. Remembered far longer than any stage fault.

**Here.** Keflavík is the only realistic international gateway; people and freight both funnel through it, with Icelandair Cargo, DHL and Silk Way West operating there (Wikipedia).

#### Front of house — the arrival building

A single staffed reception, not a gatehouse window: warm, seated, 24/7 while a unit is on, with pass issue, lockers, luggage store, hot drinks, a weather board and a live shuttle board. Bilingual staff. First and last thing anyone sees.

**Why.** A lot's culture is legible in its first thirty seconds. A cold window and a clipboard tells a visiting producer everything they need to know.

**Here.** Arrivals here are often jet-lagged, wind-battered and in darkness. Reception is a welfare function before it is a security one.

#### Concierge and production services desk

One staffed desk, 06:00–22:00, one phone number and one app thread, that will book anything: physio, a hire car, a dentist, a dog sitter, a helicopter, a table in town. Sunset Studios sells exactly this as 'concierge-style support'.

**Why.** The measure of a lot is how many separate people a production coordinator must call to get something done. One is the target.

**Here.** Visiting crew cannot navigate kennitala, Sjúkratryggingar, banking or ÁTVR hours alone. The concierge is the translation layer for an entire country.

#### Wayfinding, signage and the crew app

Bilingual IS/EN signage, wind-rated and retroreflective, legible at 30 m in sleet. One app carrying map, shuttle tracking, menus, room and physio booking, muster points, road status and the live Veðurstofa alert state. Offline-capable.

**Why.** On an 809 ha site in darkness with 400 people who arrived last week, the map is a safety system before it is a convenience.

**Here.** Veðurstofa Íslands yellow, orange and red alerts must reach every phone on the lot and automatically change the shuttle and wrap plan.

#### Sustainability as a sales asset, honestly stated

Certify to albert (BAFTA) and publish a per-shooting-day carbon figure. Geothermal district heat and a hydro/geothermal grid make heating and power near-zero-carbon, so the honest total is dominated by air freight and crew flights — publish those too.

**Why.** Major studios now score lots on sustainability inside the bid. Iceland's energy is the best story in the industry, but only if the numbers survive scrutiny.

**Here.** The grid is at capacity and Landsvirkjun has refused supply to new industrial load, so 'we run on green power' must survive a hard question about the connection.

### State of the art

#### Licensed bar and wrap social room

One 120 m² licensed social room opening only on wrap nights and events, under a municipal on-premises licence granted by Mosfellsbær with police comment (est. 8–12 weeks to obtain). No alcohol in the commissary at lunch.

**Why.** Wrap parties otherwise happen in town, cost a coach each way, and end with a drink-drive risk on an unlit road in the dark.

**Here.** Off-licence alcohol retail is a state monopoly (Vínbúðin/ÁTVR), so stock planning and pricing are not a normal supplier conversation and cannot be improvised.

#### Geothermal pool, hot pots, sauna and cold plunge

25 m four-lane outdoor geothermal pool at 29 °C; three hot pots at 38/40/42 °C; steam room, dry sauna, cold plunge; 05:30–23:00. Heated on district geothermal, so marginal heat cost is near zero. Sizing is an estimate.

**Why.** No lot in Georgia, Hertfordshire or Ontario can copy this. It is the single amenity a crew will describe to someone else unprompted.

**Here.** The swimming pool is Iceland's central social institution. A lot without a hot pot is culturally illegible to Icelandic crew, and it wastes the one thing the climate gives back for free.

#### Physiotherapy and treatment rooms

2 treatment rooms plus a rehab gym bay. Physio 3 days/week, rising to 5 whenever a stunt or water unit is working; 30-min slots bookable in the crew app. Target: same-day triage for any soft-tissue complaint.

**Why.** Grip, rigging, stunt and water work generate back and shoulder injuries constantly. Same-day physio keeps people working and cuts claims.

**Here.** Icelandic physiotherapy is partly reimbursed by Sjúkratryggingar Íslands for residents; visiting crew on A1/EHIC need a written pathway or they simply go untreated for twenty weeks.

#### Mental health provision, EAP and quiet rooms

Contracted EAP, 24/7 phone, first session within 5 working days, in Icelandic, English and Polish. 6 quiet rooms of 12–16 m², one per occupied building: lockable, no glass, dimmable, no cameras, no booking log.

**Why.** Long nights, imported crew far from family, twenty weeks in the dark. If the only private place on the lot is a parked car, you find out the hard way.

**Here.** Foreign-born residents in Reykjavík reached 26.7% by 2026, Polish the largest group (Wikipedia). Construction and locations crews need provision in their own language, not translated.

#### Sleep rooms and the tired-driver rule

8 single dark rooms (bed, blackout, background below 30 dB(A)) bookable in 2 h blocks, plus 2 driver rest rooms at the gate. Standing policy: anyone who has worked past a stated hour is offered a bed or a taxi, no questions, no cost.

**Why.** Wrap-to-drive on an unlit rural road in ice is the highest-probability fatality on any lot. A bed is cheaper than an inquest.

**Here.** Mosfellsdalur roads glaze fast, and a winter wrap at 22:00 puts tired, jet-lagged crew onto black ice in a crosswind with no streetlighting.

#### Circadian and light-therapy strategy for amenity buildings

Tuneable white throughout amenity and office space: 6,500 K and 1,000+ lux at desk 07:00–11:00, October to March, falling to 2,700 K after 18:00; target melanopic EDI 250 lx at eye (est., WELL v2 intent). Two 10,000 lux therapy alcoves in the commissary.

**Why.** Seasonal affective disorder is a scheduling risk, not a wellness nicety. Absenteeism and error rates climb through the dark months.

**Here.** Reykjavík sees under five hours of daylight in midwinter (Wikipedia). Crew can work entire weeks without seeing the sun, and imported HODs have never experienced it.

#### Salon, laundry, tailoring and parcel services

Two-chair barber and salon concession 3 days/week; dry-clean and laundry drop at 24 h turnaround; shoe repair and tailoring drop; a parcel and post room for personal deliveries. Priced at town rates, not resort rates.

**Why.** Imported HODs on a 22-week schedule have no domestic infrastructure here. Every errand they cannot do on the lot costs an hour of their day.

**Here.** Retail hours in Mosfellsbær are short and Sunday closure is common. A six-day shooting week leaves no window to do anything in town.

#### Permanent talent dressing suites

8 fixed star suites of 35–55 m² in a low-rise block behind controlled access: bath, wardrobe, private lounge, blackout, and an arrival court screened from every sightline. The fixed alternative to trailers, not a supplement.

**Why.** Trailer dressing rooms in Icelandic wind are unusable and unsafe. If the lot does not supply the alternative, the talent's deal demands one elsewhere.

**Here.** Lowland winds reach 18 m/s on 10–20 days a year (Wikipedia). A double-banger with a slide-out is a liability in that, not an amenity.

#### Auditorium and event space

250-seat flat-floor hall with retractable seating, 6 m ceiling, divisible into 3, full AV, a loading door and its own catering pantry. Used for inductions, safety briefings, table reads, press junkets, wrap events and community open days.

**Why.** Trilith Live put an 1,800-seat auditorium and two live TV stages beside the lot (Wikipedia). Event space is now a revenue line, not overhead.

**Here.** Goodwill in Mosfellsbær (population 13,715 in 2025, Wikipedia) is a planning asset. An open-day room buys tolerance for night shoots, lighting and road closures.

#### Small-tenant and co-working tier

40-desk hot-desk floor let by the desk-week to small units, VFX supervisors, agents and financiers who need a week not a building. Includes meeting credits, printing, lockers and a commissary account. Est. ISK 55,000 per desk per week.

**Why.** Not every visitor needs 2,000 m² of production office. Without a small tier they work from a hotel lobby and you never build the relationship.

**Here.** A local production or a visiting commercial is fifteen people. A lot pitched only at 400-person features has nothing to sell for forty weeks of the year.

#### Childcare — on-lot nursery

Licensed nursery to leikskóli standard, 40 places, ages 1–6, 06:30–19:30 with pre-booked extension to 22:00 on late-wrap days. Est. ratios 1:4 under two and 1:8 over three. Play yard walled to 2.4 m against wind, half of it covered.

**Why.** The gap between the end of parental leave and a municipal nursery place is the single biggest reason Icelandic crew, especially women, leave the industry.

**Here.** Municipal leikskóli places start late and close around 16:00–17:00, flatly incompatible with a twelve-hour shooting day. This is the amenity that grows the local crew base.

#### Landscape, trails and the 809 hectares

10–15 km of graded walking and running loops with wind-rated waymarkers and 3 emergency call points; a 2 km lit loop for the dark months; native revegetation with active lupine management; a dark-sky platform sited away from lot lighting.

**Why.** The land is the product. Crew who walk it become advocates; crew who only ever see a car park do not.

**Here.** Aurora on a clear winter night is a genuine hospitality asset, but only with a lighting plan that does not flood 809 hectares with sodium and kill it.

#### Days-off programme — the Iceland experience

A curated Sunday programme booked through the concierge: Sky Lagoon and Blue Lagoon blocks, glacier and highland trips, horse riding, pool tours, a standing 12-seat minibus and driver. Priced at cost, treated as retention spend.

**Why.** Crew choose a job partly for what they can do on a day off. Iceland's advantage here is enormous and almost always left completely unexploited.

**Here.** Tourism infrastructure is world-class and 20–60 minutes away, but everything books out in summer. Hold allocations in advance rather than improvising.

#### On-site crew lodge

120–180 keys in low-rise blocks: 60% studio rooms at 22 m², 30% one-bed with kitchen at 38 m², 10% two-bed family at 55 m². Weekly rates, geothermal heat, triple glazing, blackout under 1 lux, five minutes to the commissary under cover. Estimate.

**Why.** Trilith Guesthouse, a Marriott Tribute Portfolio hotel, opened at the studio gate in January 2024 (Wikipedia). Beds at the gate win shows on remote sites.

**Here.** Reykjavík hotel stock is tourist-cyclical and spikes in exactly the midnight-sun months a production wants. Buying rooms on the open market in July is brutal.

#### Relocation and immigration onboarding

A written 30-day landing programme owned by the lot, not by each production: kennitala registration, EEA or Útlendingastofnun work permit, bank account, tax card, housing, school places, a winter driving briefing and a partner introduction.

**Why.** The premise is imported HODs. The lot that removes the friction of arriving in Iceland wins the HOD, and the HOD brings the show with them.

**Here.** Non-EEA crew need permits through Útlendingastofnun; even EEA crew need a kennitala before they can be paid or sign a lease. Neither is fast unaided.

#### Partner and family landing programme

A named coordinator for accompanying partners and children: school and leikskóli placement, Icelandic classes, a partners' calendar, and gym, pool and commissary access on the crew pass. Est. 0.5 FTE — the cheapest retention line on the lot.

**Why.** HODs rarely leave because of the job. They leave because a partner is miserable in the dark in a country where they know nobody.

**Here.** Under five hours of midwinter daylight, a hard language and a twenty-week shoot the partner is not part of. Isolation is the failure mode here, not the weather.

#### Crew membership and alumni programme

A named membership on the Trilith PLUS model: pool and gym access between shows, discounted desks, first sight of upcoming bookings, a skills directory and training credits. Target: est. 60% of the Icelandic crew base enrolled within three years.

**Why.** In a national crew base near 1,000 people, the lot's real asset is its relationship with every one of them in the weeks they are not working.

**Here.** Crew who go idle between shows leave the country or leave the industry. Membership keeps them attached to your gate rather than to Copenhagen's or Dublin's.

#### Training pipeline and a film-school partnership

A formal partnership on the Cinespace–DePaul model (running since 2013, Wikipedia) or Trilith's with the Georgia Film Academy and a UGA MFA programme (2020, Wikipedia): trainee places attached to every booking, a 300 m² teaching space, est. 25 qualified new crew a year.

**Why.** You cannot book four stages at once against a 1,000-person national crew base. Either you grow crew or you fly 400 people in per show, forever.

**Here.** The highest-leverage amenity on this list: it turns the lot from a tenant of the Icelandic crew market into that market's largest single producer.

#### The site visit as a designed product

A scripted four-hour recce: an aerial pass over the 809 ha, the water stage running, the hot pots, a meal at the chef's table, the 120-seat theatre, a suite held overnight, and a leave-behind pack of real numbers. Est. target: one booking per three visits.

**Why.** Stages have commoditised and everyone's photographs look identical. The visit is where the decision is actually made.

**Here.** Getting a decision-maker to Iceland is expensive and rare. When they come, the day must be choreographed to the minute and weather-proofed twice over.

#### Measuring hospitality so the budget survives

Instrument everything: commissary covers per day, coffee cups per day, gym unique users per week, pool entries, shuttle load factor, quiet-room occupancy, physio wait, concierge tickets and time-to-close, wrap survey (est. NPS target 60+), rebooking rate (target 50%+ in 24 months).

**Why.** Amenity budgets get cut first because nobody measures them. Numbers turn the commissary from a cost line into the reason for the rebooking.

**Here.** With no meaningful domestic market, repeat international bookings are survival. Rebooking rate is the only number that ultimately matters here.

### What gets got wrong

- RESEARCH LIMIT: this session's WebSearch budget (200/200 calls) was already exhausted before I started, so all research was done by direct WebFetch of named sites. Several benchmark pages returned 404 or 503 and could not be read — Sunset Las Palmas, Cinespace amenities, Warner Bros Studio Facilities, Origo Budapest, Trilith's wellness-centre page, filminiceland's why-Iceland page. Those lots are therefore under-cited relative to Trilith and Shinfield.
- WHAT IS ATTRIBUTED vs ESTIMATED: every m², seat count, key count, headcount, ratio, price and target in this spec is my estimate unless a source is named in the field. Attributed facts come from: Wikipedia (Trilith Studios, Town at Trilith, Shepperton, Sky Studios Elstree, Pinewood Toronto, Cinespace, Keflavík International Airport, Reykjavík, Mosfellsbær, Strætó bs, Climate of Iceland); trilithstudios.com and trilithguesthouse.com; shadowboxstudios.com/shinfield; sunsetstudios.com; filminiceland.com/incentives.
- ICELAND VAT was not verified in this session. I believe the standard rate is 24% with a reduced rate around 11% covering food and accommodation, but confirm the current schedule with Skatturinn before pricing meals, lodge keys or event space. Commissary subsidy maths depends on it.
- ICELANDIC PARENTAL LEAVE length (12 months shared between parents, roughly 6 each with a transferable portion, since 2021) was not verified this session. The lactation-room and childcare items lean on it; confirm with Vinnumálastofnun / Fæðingarorlofssjóður.
- REBATE ELIGIBILITY OF HOSPITALITY SPEND: filminiceland.com states eligible costs are those deductible under Iceland's Income Tax Act and directly incurred in Iceland during pre-production, principal photography or post. That strongly implies lot-supplied catering, housing and transport are claimable, but it is not stated explicitly. Get written confirmation from the Reimbursement Committee per booking rather than promising it in a bid.
- THE 35% TEST HAS THREE LIMBS and a production can clear two and fail the third. The 50-crew limb requires Icelandic-TAXED salaries for 50+ crew over 50+ working days (filminiceland.com), which directly conflicts with a model where most HODs are imported on foreign payroll. The housing strategy and the rebate strategy pull against each other and must be modelled together, not separately.
- THE 2028 CLIFF: the scheme is legislated through 31 December 2028 (filminiceland.com) and has been renewed regularly since 1999. Any lodge, nursery, pool or training investment with a payback longer than about four years is being underwritten by a renewal that has not happened. Price that risk explicitly.
- MUNICIPAL AND REGIONAL LICENSING, not national: the alcohol licence (Mosfellsbær with police comment), food premises (regional Heilbrigðiseftirlit), and the nursery all run on their own clocks and none of them run on a studio's programme. Start them at planning stage, not fit-out.
- THE NURSERY IS THE HARDEST ITEM HERE. Icelandic nursery licensing, staff qualifications and ratios are set nationally and locally, staff must speak Icelandic, and a 22:00 pickup may not be permittable at all. Assume 12–18 months to licence and treat the extended hours as a target, not a certainty. My stated ratios (1:4 under two, 1:8 over three) are estimates against Icelandic norms, not quoted regulation.
- ANIMAL IMPORT: Iceland has one of the strictest regimes in Europe — import permit plus isolation for dogs and cats. A visiting production cannot fly a picture dog in at short notice. Verify current requirements and quarantine duration with MAST before promising anything.
- AMENITY ELECTRICAL LOAD is small next to stage load but not zero: pool plant, kitchens, gym, lodge, EV charging, snow-melt pumps — est. 1.5–2.5 MW at peak for the whole amenity estate. Given that the grid is at capacity and Landsvirkjun has refused supply to new industrial load, this has to sit inside whatever connection is actually secured. Verify against the connection agreement, not against a wish.
- ON-SITE MEDICAL is a commercial arrangement, not a regulated ambulance service. The interface with 112, Landspítali and the Coast Guard's shared SAR/HEMS helicopter needs to be documented in writing with response-time expectations, not assumed. Helicopter EMS can be grounded by the same wind that makes you need it.
- SCREENING-ROOM NUMBERS (ISO 2969 B-chain, 85 dB(C) reference at -20 dBFS pink, 48 cd/m² screen white, NC-20 background, RT60 under 0.4 s) are standard cinema practice from memory and were not re-verified against current SMPTE/DCI documents this session. Check before tender.
- MELANOPIC EDI 250 lx at eye is a design intent aligned to WELL v2 circadian lighting thinking, not a verified compliance threshold. WELL's actual option thresholds were not checked this session.
- THE ~1,000 NATIONAL CREW FIGURE comes from the brief, not from a source I verified. The training, membership, casting and staffing items all scale off it. If the real figure is materially different, the training pipeline sizing changes first.
- WIND DESIGN: 18 m/s on 10–20 lowland days a year is a national average (Wikipedia, Climate of Iceland). Site-specific design wind for Mosfellsdalur will be higher and must come from a met mast or Veðurstofa data. Every canopy rating, blind rating, shelter-belt and sheltered-terrace decision in this document depends on a number I do not have.
- SCOPE BOUNDARY: I have deliberately specified nothing inside a stage envelope. Where an item touches a stage — kiosks at stage clusters, drying rooms per cluster, covered links to stage doors, the buggy route to a talent suite — the stage-side interface belongs to the other pass and the two need reconciling at the door threshold.
- SEISMIC: pool structures, hot pots, tall commissary kitchen equipment and the lodge all need seismic restraint that a Georgia or Hertfordshire equivalent does not. This was not specified item by item here and should be a single engineering pass across the whole amenity estate.
- VOLCANIC ASH: an ash event closes Keflavík, which severs the airport transfer, the hotel block and the HOD arrival programme simultaneously. The hospitality plan needs a documented ash contingency — extended lodge capacity, sea-freight fallback, a hold-in-place provisioning stock — that I have flagged but not sized.

---

## 12. Sugar Land Studios, Mosfellsbær

*the lot as landlord and as an asset: tenancy models, lease anatomy, insurance, Icelandic tax and title, valuation, debt, and the downside case*

### Essential

#### Unit of sale: the stage-week, not the square metre

Price per stage per 5-day week; 7-day multiplier ~1.15, holdover 1.5x, prep/strike at 60-70% of shoot rate (all estimates). Broker range for purpose-built stage space (estimate): UK £1.00-1.75/sq ft/wk, Atlanta $0.70-1.10, LA $2.00+. At £1.50/sq ft/wk the 6,000 m2 room bills ~£97,000/week.

**Why.** Every downstream number — occupancy, NOI, valuation, covenant headroom — is derived from stage-weeks sold. Get the unit wrong and the model is wrong.

**Here.** Quote in EUR or USD. ISK is illiquid, the tenant budgets in USD, and there is no Icelandic stage comparable — RVK Studios at Gufunes is the only rival and publishes nothing.

#### Booking as a licence, not a lease

A shoot booking must be a licence to occupy: no exclusive possession of the whole demise, landlord retains keys, master access and the right to relocate within the lot, term under 12 months, cancellable on notice with a stepped cancellation ladder (e.g. 100% inside 30 days, 50% at 31-90 days — estimate).

**Why.** A booking that reads as a lease imports statutory notice periods and restoration arguments you never priced into a 6-week hold.

**Here.** Húsaleigulög nr. 36/1994 art. 2(2): the Act's commercial-premises provisions are frávíkjanleg (derogable). You must contract out clause by clause in writing; silence imports the statute, including art. 56 notice of 6/9/12 months by tenure.

#### Streamer master lease: the demise schedule

Schedule every let element separately with plans and areas: stages (GIA m2, clear height, door sizes), workshops, production offices (m2 and desk count), parking bays, basecamp hardstanding, backlot hectares, tank, and rights over common estate. Name what is common and what is exclusive.

**Why.** Disputes on studio leases are almost never about rent; they are about whether the mill, the paint shop, 40 parking bays and the road to the backlot were inside the demise.

**Here.** Comparables: Amazon Prime Video took Shepperton 'for the next decade' (Guardian, 9 Feb 2022); NBCUniversal leases and operates Gray Television's 43-acre Assembly Atlanta (announced Jun 2022).

#### Term, break options and WAULT

Target 10-15 years certain on the anchor, staggered so no more than one third of income breaks in any 24 months. Track WAULT to break and WAULT to expiry separately. A tenant-only break at year 5 on 12 months' notice with a 6-9 month rent penalty is the realistic landing zone (estimate).

**Why.** Valuation is driven by WAULT to break, not to expiry. A 15-year lease with a year-5 tenant break is a 5-year asset to any buyer or lender.

#### The 2028 rebate cliff clause

Iceland's reimbursement legislation is valid through 31 December 2028 (filminiceland.com). Define a 'Rebate Event' — rate falling below 25%, or non-renewal — and set the consequence: tenant break on 12 months' notice with a break premium, or a stepped rent reduction, and a landlord break if renewal comes with a rent uplift.

**Why.** Every lease longer than three years is a bet on legislation with a published sunset. Unpriced, the whole rent roll reprices to zero on one Althingi vote.

**Here.** The scheme is approved EEA state aid; renewal is a political and an ESA question, not just a budget line. Assume a 12-18 month decision lag before expiry and diarise the lobbying calendar from 2027.

#### Rent review: CPI indexation with collar and cap

Icelandic commercial standard is annual indexation to vísitala neysluverðs (CPI), reviewed monthly or annually. Add a collar and cap (e.g. 0% floor, 5% ceiling — estimate) and a hard open-market review at years 5 and 10 with an upward-only provision and expert determination in default of agreement.

**Why.** Iceland has run double-digit CPI inside living memory; an uncapped index clause is a real risk to a tenant and an uncollared one leaves the landlord exposed in deflation.

**Here.** Residential letting is a different regime: the residential provisions of Húsaleigulög 36/1994 are not derogable, and 2024 amendments tightened registration and rent-increase rules. Take local advice before applying the same index to crew housing.

#### Currency, FX and where the risk sits

Denominate rent in EUR or USD with payment in ISK at a stated fixing (Central Bank of Iceland mid-rate, two business days prior), or in ISK with an FX collar. State which index applies to which currency: CPI indexation of a EUR rent double-counts inflation and is a drafting error.

**Why.** Costs are ISK (rates, staff, heat, snow), debt may be ISK or EUR, income is USD. Unmatched, a 15% ISK move wipes a year of NOI.

**Here.** A hard-currency lease is the thing that unlocks hard-currency debt: Icelandic lenders will price FX borrowing against matched FX revenue. This is the single strongest structuring reason to sign a USD master lease.

#### Service charge: code, budget, cap, certification

Adopt the RICS professional statement 'Service charges in commercial property' (1st ed., effective 1 April 2019) contractually: no profit to landlord, budget issued at least one month before the year starts, certified accounts within four months of year end, apportionment matrix disclosed, and a cap (e.g. CPI+2%).

**Why.** Studio estate charges are heavy — roads, gatehouse, substation, tank water treatment, snow. Uncapped and uncertified, they become the first thing a sophisticated tenant refuses to pay.

**Here.** There is no Icelandic equivalent code, so it has to be imported by contract. Budget separately for storm damage and snow clearance; these are frequency items here, not exceptions.

#### Repair standard and schedule of condition

Full repairing and insuring on the anchor, limited by a photographic and drone schedule of condition annexed at grant and updated at each anniversary. Define the standard as 'no worse than the recorded condition, fair wear from the permitted use excepted'.

**Why.** An FRI lease on a two-year-old building without a schedule of condition means the tenant hands back a building better than they received it — and argues about it for a year.

**Here.** Húsaleigulög art. 63 default: return in the same condition, fair wear excepted. Derogable, so write your own standard, but know the fallback if you fail to.

#### Dilapidations, yield-up and set strike

Separate three obligations: strike (sets, rigs, cabling, floor paint, anchors made good to a stated floor tolerance, e.g. +/-3 mm over 3 m); reinstatement of tenant alterations at landlord election on 6 months' notice; and repair. Secure with a reinstatement bond or deposit (estimate: 3 months' rent or EUR 40/m2).

**Why.** Set strike is the most-breached obligation on any lot. Left unsecured, the landlord inherits 2,000 tonnes of scenery and a 6-week void before the next let.

**Here.** Waste disposal is expensive and constrained; specify who pays for shipping scenery waste and hazardous materials, and require a waste-transfer record per strike.

#### Alienation: assignment, underletting, group sharing, change of control

Permit sharing with group companies and with production SPVs on notice (streamers shoot through single-purpose vehicles); prohibit assignment without consent, not to be unreasonably withheld, subject to covenant test; make a change of control of a non-group tenant a deemed assignment.

**Why.** A streamer that cannot put a production SPV into the space will not sign. A landlord who lets the covenant walk on a share sale has lost the asset's value.

#### Covenant strength and security for rent

Underwrite the actual contracting entity, not the brand. Require a parent guarantee from the listed group, or an on-demand bank guarantee / standby LC of 6-12 months' gross rent plus service charge. Icelandic market practice is a bankaábyrgð of 3-6 months (estimate); a specialised lot should demand more.

**Why.** Production SPVs are formed to be insolvent on wrap. Without a parent or a bank behind them the rent roll is decorative.

**Here.** An Icelandic bank guarantee is enforceable locally and quickly; a foreign parent guarantee needs an Icelandic-law submission clause and a service-of-process agent, or you are litigating in Delaware.

#### Compulsory fire insurance and the brunabótamat gap

Lög nr. 48/1994 art. 1: the owner must fire-insure every building. The sum insured is brunabótamat, a statutory reinstatement valuation set free of charge by HMS (Húsnæðis- og mannvirkjastofnun) under art. 2; owners may commission a revaluation at their own cost.

**Why.** Brunabótamat is a standardised model. For a 30 m stage with a 60-tonne grid it will sit well below true reinstatement cost, and that gap is uninsured unless you top it up.

**Here.** Buy a separate all-risks policy for the difference between brunabótamat and full reinstatement including debris removal, professional fees, code upgrade and 24-36 months of delay. Request an HMS revaluation at practical completion.

#### NTÍ natural catastrophe cover — what it is and what it is not

Lög nr. 55/1992: cover attaches automatically to every fire-insured building and chattel. Perils (art. 4): eruption, earthquake, landslide, avalanche, flood. Premium (art. 11) 0.25 per mille of sum insured on property, 0.20 per mille on listed infrastructure. Deductible 2% of each claim, min ISK 400,000 on buildings (art. 10).

**Why.** It is cheap, automatic and statutory — and it is also capped, deductible-heavy and covers physical damage only. Assuming it is a full seismic and volcanic solution is the classic error.

**Here.** Art. 18 caps NTÍ's total liability per event at a permille of aggregate sums insured in force (consolidated text quotes 7.5 per mille); above that, all claims are scaled down pro rata. A national-scale event pays you a fraction.

#### Movables are only covered if separately fire-insured

NTÍ art. 5(1) attaches to items that carry fire insurance. A tenant's sets, costumes, camera and lighting packages, and an LED volume held as tenant chattels have no catastrophe cover unless fire-insured in Iceland. Make Icelandic fire cover on tenant contents a lease obligation with evidence of cover each year.

**Why.** An eruption that damages EUR 12m of tenant kit with no NTÍ attachment turns straight into a claim against the landlord and a fight about the lease.

**Here.** Foreign production insurance placed in London or Los Angeles does not automatically create the Icelandic fire policy that NTÍ attaches to. Check the local policy exists, not just the certificate.

#### Loss of rent, business interruption and the volcanic exclusion

Landlord buys loss of rent with an indemnity period of 36 months, not the default 12 — imported steel and weather-dependent sea freight make a 24-month rebuild realistic. Include denial of access, utilities failure, and damage to neighbouring property.

**Why.** Rebuild time, not rebuild cost, is what kills a levered studio. A 12-month indemnity period on a 24-month rebuild is a default event.

**Here.** NTÍ indemnifies physical damage only and pays no consequential loss. Volcanic and seismic business interruption must be bought commercially and will be sub-limited or excluded — expect to self-insure part of it (estimate).

#### The insurance matrix and waiver of subrogation

A one-page schedule in the lease: landlord insures structure, common plant, estate liability, loss of rent, terrorism; tenant insures contents, tenant improvements, production package (cast, negative, extra expense), public liability (min EUR 10m), employer's liability, marine cargo, drone. Mutual waiver of subrogation.

**Why.** Without a subrogation waiver the landlord's insurer pays for a set fire and then sues the production that caused it — and the tenant never comes back.

#### Power: capacity reservation and take-or-pay

Mirror the landlord's own supply and connection agreements with Veitur and Landsnet down into the lease: reserved MVA per tenant, take-or-pay on the reservation whether drawn or not, metering and recharge at cost plus a stated margin (estimate: 0-5%), and curtailment rights in the order the upstream contract imposes them.

**Why.** Power is the scarcest thing on this lot. Unreserved, the second tenant discovers there is none; unpriced, the landlord funds standing charges for capacity nobody uses.

**Here.** Landsvirkjun has restricted deliveries to large users and the transmission system is constrained. A grid allocation once surrendered may be unobtainable — treat contracted MVA as an asset on the balance sheet, not a utility account.

#### Default, forfeiture and enforcement in Iceland

Define monetary default (rent unpaid 14 days after written demand), insolvency events, and material breach with a 30-day cure. Remedies: draw on the guarantee, default interest at the statutory rate (dráttarvextir set by the Central Bank), termination, and possession via útburður through the district commissioner.

**Why.** An Icelandic possession action on a contested commercial lease takes months. Cash security and a clean termination mechanic are worth more than the right to sue.

**Here.** Icelandic default interest (dráttarvextir) is fixed periodically by the Central Bank and is punitive by design — state that it applies, and to service charge as well as rent.

#### VAT: voluntary registration and the twenty-year clawback

Icelandic VAT is 24%; letting real property is exempt, but voluntary registration (frjáls skráning) is available for non-residential commercial letting, which makes the rent taxable and the input VAT on construction recoverable. Input VAT on a EUR 180m build is roughly EUR 35m (estimate) — the single largest cashflow item on the project.

**Why.** Stay exempt and EUR 35m of VAT becomes build cost. Register and then change use or deregister inside the adjustment period and you repay it in slices.

**Here.** Real-property input tax is subject to an adjustment (leiðrétting) period under the VAT regulations — commonly cited as 20 years for buildings. Confirm the current period and the pro-rata formula with RSK before any deregistration, sale or change of use.

#### Property tax and the cost of an empty lot

Fasteignaskattur under lög nr. 4/1995 art. 3: commercial and industrial property is category C, up to 1.32% of fasteignamat, and a municipality may add a surcharge of up to 25% of that rate — a 1.65% ceiling. Add lóðarleiga, vatnsgjald, fráveitugjald and waste charges on top.

**Why.** It is charged on assessed value, not on income. At a plausible ISK 20bn assessment (estimate) that is ISK 264-330m a year whether or not a single stage is let.

**Here.** Confirm Mosfellsbær's actual C-class rate and surcharge in its álagningarhlutföll. Iceland has no equivalent of UK empty-rates relief; challenge the fasteignamat instead — a specialised studio has no comparables and the model will be wrong.

#### Depreciation: component-split the build

Income Tax Act 90/2003 straight-line bands: commercial and office buildings 1-3%; factory buildings, garages and warehouses 3-6%; machinery 10-30%; office equipment and vehicles 20-35%; boreholes, powerlines and work camps 7.5-10%; goodwill 10-20%. The taxpayer elects within the band.

**Why.** Every euro argued out of the 1-3% shell and into the 10-30% plant pool is cash. On a EUR 180m build a 10-point reclassification shift is worth millions a year.

**Here.** Argue the stage envelope as a warehouse/factory building at 3-6%, not office at 1-3%. Grid and rigging, HV distribution, HVAC, gensets, tank filtration and water heating should sit in plant at 10-30%.

#### Related-party rent, transfer pricing and the rebate

If the family owns both the lot and the production company, the intercompany stage rent must be arm's length and documented. Iceland's rebate covers costs deductible under the Income Tax Act incurred in Iceland — rent qualifies, but inflated related-party rent is the first thing an auditor tests.

**Why.** Recovering 25-35% of an inflated internal rent is the fastest way to lose the rebate, the audit and the scheme's goodwill for every future production.

**Here.** Set the internal rate against a published external rate card, let at least one arm's-length third-party production a year to evidence market rent, and keep the benchmarking file.

#### Which valuation method applies — and it is not one

Let to a strong covenant on a long lease: investment method, capitalise net rent at a market yield. Owner-operated four-wall: trade-related property under RICS Red Book VPGA 4, valued on maintainable EBITDA and a multiple. Specialised and untested with no market: depreciated replacement cost.

**Why.** The three methods can be a factor of two apart on the same building. Whoever picks the method sets the price.

**Here.** With no Icelandic studio comparables a valuer will default to DRC and a lender will discount that further. A signed master lease is what moves the asset from DRC into the investment method — that is its real value.

#### Yield on cost versus exit yield: the development gap

Estimate: build at EUR 3,500-5,500/m2 for stages here (imported structure, seismic detailing, weather-delayed sea freight), so 13,200 m2 of stage plus ~15,000 m2 of support, roads, substation, water treatment and tank lands at EUR 180-260m. Stage revenue at 65% occupancy is roughly EUR 6-8m; total NOI perhaps EUR 7-9m.

**Why.** That is a 3-4.5% yield on cost against an Icelandic required yield plausibly 6.5-8%. The asset is worth materially less than it costs to build, on day one.

**Here.** This is the central financial fact of the project and it is not a failure of design. Studio lots are not justified by rent; they are justified by trading income, by the land, and by a strategic owner who wants the capacity.

#### The buyer's due diligence list

WAULT to break and expiry; covenant and guarantor accounts; rent versus ERV; capex backlog and reserve balance; contracted MVA and whether the supply agreement transfers; TPN report; seismic PML study; brunabótamat versus reinstatement; title, registered leases and charges; ESA position on any aid; operator contract and its assignability.

**Why.** Everything a buyer cannot verify becomes a price chip or a retention. Assembling this pack before you need it is worth more than any marketing.

**Here.** Two Icelandic items a foreign buyer will not know to ask for and will panic about late: the NTÍ event cap, and whether the power allocation survives a change of control.

#### Debt: sizing, covenants and currency matching

Expect a single-asset, single-sector, no-comparable facility: LTV capped at 50-60%, interest cover 2.0x+, debt service cover 1.3x+, a cash-trap trigger, and amortisation from day one (estimates). Iceland's lending market is essentially three banks — Landsbankinn, Íslandsbanki and Arion.

**Why.** Covenant headroom, not the margin, is what determines whether you survive a year at 40% occupancy. Model the covenants at the downside case before signing.

**Here.** Choose deliberately between verðtryggt (CPI-linked) and óverðtryggt debt, and match currency to the lease. A USD master lease supports USD debt; an ISK-indexed loan against USD rent is an unhedged short position dressed as a mortgage.

#### A studio business and a property business are different animals

A property business sells space: rent times area, measured by WAULT, covenant and yield, with near-zero operating leverage. A studio business sells time and service: occupancy times rate plus ancillary, with a hotel's cost base and a hotel's operating leverage. This lot is both, and they pull in opposite directions.

**Why.** Confusing them is the commonest strategic error on a new lot: a landlord's instincts say sign the longest lease to the strongest covenant, which caps upside and hands the business to the tenant.

#### The 809 hectares are a separate asset with a separate strategy

Roughly 3-4% of the land will be built on. The rest is a land bank whose value turns entirely on planning. Under Jarðalög nr. 81/2004 art. 5-6 the municipality decides whether land leaves agricultural use, weighing whether the area sought exceeds need and whether poorer land could serve instead.

**Why.** The land, not the buildings, is where the value is created and where the downside is cushioned. Farmland and consented development land differ by an order of magnitude.

**Here.** Ministerial consent under art. 10a bites where a buyer and related parties would hold five or more registered farms over 50 ha, or 1,500 ha in total. At 809 ha you are under it — but any adjacent acquisition should be tested against it first.

#### Occupancy benchmarks and the honest downside case

FilmLA reported certified Los Angeles soundstage occupancy at 63% in 2024, an all-time low (Variety and The Hollywood Reporter, 3 Apr 2025), still roughly one third empty through the first half of 2025 (Variety and Deadline, 18 Mar 2026), against 90%+ before 2020.

**Why.** The mature, deepest market on earth runs at 63%. A new lot in a market of a thousand crew must be underwritten at well below that in years 1-3.

**Here.** Model three cases: base 55-60% from year 4, downside 25-35% through a strike or rebate lapse, and a zero case. The zero case is the one that sets your minimum cash reserve.

#### What actually happens when the work stops

Fixed costs do not stop: property tax on assessed value, compulsory fire insurance, security, statutory inspections, heat and grounds. Variable revenue goes to near zero in weeks. The sequence is: rate-card discounting, then term deals at any price, then staff reduction, then deferred capex, then covenant breach.

**Why.** Recognising the sequence early buys the one thing that matters — the option to wait. Studios that failed mostly failed at the covenant, not at the rate card.

**Here.** Precedents to study: the scrapped Sunset Waltham Cross scheme (Mar 2025); LA at 63% occupancy; and Pinewood exiting Trilith in 2019, selling its stake to the local partner (The Citizen and AJC, 21 Aug 2019) — the family fortune stayed, the operator left.

### Expected

#### Rent structure: base, steps, free period, overage

Headline base rent, stepped 2-4% a year or CPI-linked; rent-free of 3-6 months per 5 years of term as fit-out and incentive (estimate); plus turnover overage — e.g. 2-4% of the tenant's qualifying Icelandic spend above a threshold, or a per-shoot-day surcharge (design proposal, estimate).

**Why.** Overage is how a landlord participates in a good year without repricing the base in a bad one. Pure base rent gives you a bond with none of a studio's upside.

**Here.** Rent paid to an Icelandic propco is a cost deductible under the Income Tax Act incurred in Iceland, so it is itself eligible spend — a qualifying production recovers 25-35% of its rent. Net effective rent is 65-75% of headline.

#### Sinking fund and capex reserve

Hold a reserve fund built at an agreed rate per m2 per year (estimate: EUR 8-15/m2/yr on stages), in a designated account, itemised against a 25-year lifecycle plan: roof membranes, rigging recertification, HV switchgear, tank liner, road surfaces, perimeter fencing.

**Why.** Without a reserve the landlord funds a EUR 6m roof replacement out of one year's NOI, or defers it and takes the hit on exit as a capex deduction.

**Here.** Check the tax treatment: a reserve collected from tenants is generally taxable income to the landlord unless genuinely held on trust. Get a ruling before pricing it into the service charge.

#### Alterations, tenant fit-out and who owns the volume

Licence to alter for anything touching structure, grid, HV, fire strategy or the tank. State ownership of major installed kit — an LED volume, a permanent water heating plant — and whether it is landlord fixture, tenant chattel, or transfers at a stated residual on expiry.

**Why.** A EUR 8-15m LED volume that nobody owns on paper is the thing that stops a sale, a refinancing or a handback.

#### Damage, abatement and the termination trigger

Rent and service charge abate in the proportion the damaged area bears to the demise, from the date of damage, while loss-of-rent insurance responds. Either party may terminate if reinstatement is not practicable within 24 months, or if insurance proceeds are irrecoverable other than through that party's default.

**Why.** An abatement clause with no long-stop leaves both sides bound to a dead building for years while the lender's ICR covenant breaches.

#### Registration of title and of the lease

Register (þinglýsa) the master lease at the sýslumaður so it binds a purchaser and takes a dated priority against later charges. Stamp duty under lög nr. 138/2013 falls on transfers of ownership: 0.8% for individuals, 1.6% for legal entities (art. 5); leases and mortgage deeds are not in the charging article.

**Why.** An unregistered lease can be defeated by a buyer or a mortgagee. Registering it is cheap and is what makes the income stream survive a sale.

**Here.** Register the lot's plots with their own fastanúmer first; you cannot register a lease against a demise that does not exist in the property register.

#### Loss carryforward has a ten-year fuse

Icelandic tax losses carry forward ten years from the year incurred, and there is no carryback. Heavy early depreciation plus interest on a EUR 100m+ facility produces large losses in years 1-6 that must be absorbed by year 10 or they expire unused.

**Why.** A tax shield that expires unused is the same as never having had it — and it silently shifts the after-tax IRR of the whole project.

**Here.** Model the loss expiry schedule alongside the lease profile. If break options cluster in years 5-7, the losses and the income are misaligned by design.

#### Interest limitation and thin capitalisation

Net interest deduction is capped at 30% of tax EBITDA once interest expense exceeds ISK 100 million a year. Exemptions: intra-group interest within an Icelandic joint-taxation group, and where the taxpayer's equity ratio is within 2 percentage points of the consolidated group ratio.

**Why.** A shareholder loan from the family holding company is the obvious way to fund this, and it is exactly what the rule catches at a EUR 100m+ debt quantum.

**Here.** Transfer pricing documentation is mandatory above ISK 1bn operating revenue, must be kept 7 years, and must be produced within 45 days on request. Price the shareholder loan and the intercompany lease at arm's length from day one.

#### Holding structure and leakage on the way out

Non-resident withholding: dividends 20%, interest 13%, royalties 20%. Dividends to an EEA company are effectively nil — the 20% is withheld and reimbursed on filing the following year. Corporate income tax on an ehf is 20%; other legal forms are taxed at 37.6%.

**Why.** Where the propco sits determines whether the cash reaches the family or sits behind a 20% tollgate and a one-year timing drag.

**Here.** Keep the propco as an Icelandic ehf with an EEA parent, not a partnership. And test whether a US streamer's occupation and personnel create an Icelandic permanent establishment under the Iceland-US treaty — they will ask.

#### Accounting election: investment property or PP&E

If the lot is let to third parties it is investment property under IAS 40 and may be carried at fair value, with revaluation running through P&L. If the owner occupies and operates it, it is PP&E under IAS 16 at depreciated cost. Mixed use is split by area.

**Why.** The election decides whether you report valuation gains, what your covenant LTV is measured against, and how a buyer reads your accounts.

#### IFRS 16 is why the tenant wants a break

A 10-year non-cancellable master lease at EUR 10m a year lands roughly a EUR 75m right-of-use asset and lease liability on the tenant's balance sheet (estimate at a 5% discount rate). A break option shortens the lease term used in the calculation and shrinks the liability.

**Why.** Understanding this converts a negotiation about breaks into a negotiation about price — sell the break for a premium rather than losing the term for free.

#### Read the comparable transactions honestly

TPG Real Estate bought Cinespace for a reported USD 1.0-1.1bn in Nov 2021 (THR, Chicago Tribune, 12 Nov 2021). Legal & General funded and developed the 32-acre Sky Studios Elstree, reported at £230m, with Sky as occupier (L&G releases Dec 2019/Jul 2020; Construction Enquirer, 9 Jul 2020).

**Why.** These set the pricing frame a buyer arrives with. They were also all struck in a demand cycle that has since turned.

**Here.** The counter-example matters more: the consented £700m Sunset Waltham Cross scheme was declared 'no longer feasible' and scrapped in March 2025 (Screen Daily, 21 Mar 2025). Consent and capital were not enough.

#### The security package and lender step-in

First-ranking veðréttur over the registered property; assignment of leases and rental income; pledge over the propco shares; account pledges over the rent and reserve accounts; insurances assigned with the lender as loss payee; and a direct agreement with the operator.

**Why.** A lender with no step-in rights over the operating platform cannot sell a working studio — only an empty shed — so it lends less.

**Here.** Agree a non-disturbance and attornment agreement with the anchor tenant: the tenant keeps possession on enforcement provided it is not in default. No streamer signs a 15-year lease behind a mortgage without one.

#### Sale and leaseback versus forward funding

Sale and leaseback sells the built lot to an institution at a yield and takes back a 20-25 year index-linked lease. Forward funding has the institution buy the land and fund construction against a fixed-price design-and-build contract for a developer profit on cost of roughly 10-15% (estimate).

**Why.** Both release capital. Sale and leaseback converts a flexible owned asset into a fixed liability that you still owe through a trough — which is when studios trough.

**Here.** Sky Studios Elstree is the forward-funded shape: L&G owns and funds, Sky occupies. Given a self-funded family balance sheet, the live question is whether to take an institution in at all, or only at refinancing.

#### Income concentration limits

Set a policy: no single tenant above 40% of gross income after year three, at least three income-producing tenants, and a deliberate mix of master lease, term deal and four-wall (estimates). Track it monthly as a covenant to yourself.

**Why.** A single-tenant specialised asset is priced as one credit. When that credit cuts commissioning, the lot's value moves in one step, not gradually.

#### Subdivide so you can sell or mortgage one building

Register each stage, workshop block and office building as its own property unit with a fastanúmer, with plot plans and, where a building contains separately owned parts, an eignaskiptayfirlýsing apportioning shares in common parts and costs.

**Why.** You cannot sell, mortgage or separately let a building that does not exist as a registered property. Retrofitting the subdivision later takes months and needs every chargeholder's consent.

**Here.** Do it at practical completion while the as-built drawings, the surveyor and the municipality are all still engaged. It is trivial then and expensive in year six.

#### Crew housing is a different, protected asset class

Housing imported heads of department turns the owner into a residential landlord. The residential provisions of Húsaleigulög nr. 36/1994 are not derogable, unlike the commercial ones, and the 2024 amendments tightened lease registration and rent-increase rules.

**Why.** Applying a commercial lease template to crew housing produces unenforceable terms, and the CPI clause you rely on commercially may not survive.

**Here.** Structure it as service accommodation licensed to the production, or as a corporate let to the tenant company with the tenant handling occupation — do not create dozens of direct protected tenancies with individual crew.

#### Alternative use is what sets the floor

Rank the conversion options by the capex to get there: cold storage and logistics (15 minutes from Reykjavík), events and indoor sport, geothermal-heated controlled-environment agriculture, and data centre. Model each as a residual land value, net of demolition and abnormals.

**Why.** The residual value of the site in its best alternative use is the real floor under the asset, and it is what a lender's valuer will stress the loan against.

**Here.** The data centre option is the highest-value one and it is gated entirely by contracted power. A constrained grid simultaneously makes the option valuable and makes it unavailable to anyone who let their allocation lapse.

#### State aid, ESA and anything the municipality offers

Any land, infrastructure, tax abatement or below-cost service from Mosfellsbær or the state is state aid under Article 61 of the EEA Agreement and is notifiable to the EFTA Surveillance Authority. Act nr. 41/2015 on incentives for initial investments has been repealed; pre-1 July 2020 agreements survive for their stated terms.

**Why.** Unnotified aid can be clawed back with interest years later, from the recipient. A support package assumed into the business plan and then unwound is a solvency event.

**Here.** There is currently no general statutory incentive regime to lean on. Any Mosfellsbær package is bespoke, must be state-aid cleared, and should be assumed to take 12-18 months.

### State of the art

#### Taking security over the production rebate

Take an assignment by way of security of the tenant's reimbursement claim against the Icelandic Film Centre, with notice to the Centre and a payment direction. Rebate is applied for before production starts and paid after completion and audit — the classic gap financiers already collateralise.

**Why.** It is the one asset a production SPV reliably has. Perfecting it converts a worthless covenant into a secured one.

**Here.** 25% base with no minimum spend; 35% where Icelandic spend is at least ISK 350m with 30+ working days (10+ shooting) and 50+ crew for 50+ days; 35% automatically for children's and youth content (filminiceland.com).

#### Content security accreditation as a landlord obligation

Maintain MPA Trusted Partner Network (TPN) assessment for the lot's physical security: access control with audited logs, CCTV coverage and retention, restricted zones, visitor management, asset-handling procedure, and a named security lead. Renew annually and warrant it in the lease.

**Why.** A major will not put unreleased content on a lot that is not assessed. It has moved from nice-to-have to a condition of letting, and a buyer's DD now asks for the report.

#### Green lease clauses and the carbon proposition

Data-sharing obligation (metered energy, water, waste by tenant by month), a cooperation clause on efficiency works with a cost/benefit split, a prohibition on diesel generation except for documented failure, and support for the tenant's BAFTA albert certification and Ad Green style reporting.

**Why.** A streamer's sustainability team now sits in the leasing decision. Carbon data you cannot produce is a clause you cannot sign.

**Here.** Electricity is hydro and geothermal and space heat is district geothermal. Quantify it — kgCO2e per stage-week — and sell it. It is the most defensible differentiator this lot has against Shinfield, Trilith or Origo.

#### Owner and operator are two different businesses

Propco holds title, debt and the leases. Opco holds the rate card, the bookings, the crew, the vendor relationships and the reputation, under a management agreement — hotel-style base fee of 2-4% of revenue plus an incentive of 10-20% of EBITDA above a hurdle (estimates).

**Why.** Split them and the asset becomes institutionally saleable without selling the business, and the operating team can be replaced without touching the title.

**Here.** The Hackman Capital Partners model — owning both the real estate and, via MBS, the operating platform — is the template. It is also what lets an owner take the lot back in-house if the operator underperforms.

#### The mothball protocol

Keep heat on and humidity below 60% RH, dehumidify the tank hall or drain and passivate the tank, exercise winches and chain hoists quarterly under load, maintain fire detection and suppression certification, keep insurance in force and disclose vacancy, and maintain a 24/7 gatehouse. Estimate: EUR 4-6m a year to own an empty lot.

**Why.** A mothballed lot that loses its certifications and its accreditation cannot reopen in weeks when the work returns — it reopens in a year, having missed the recovery.

**Here.** The first rule is: do not surrender the grid allocation. Cheap geothermal heat makes Icelandic mothballing unusually affordable; the power contract is the thing that cannot be bought back.

### What gets got wrong

- Verify the current text of Lög nr. 55/1992 art. 18 — the NTÍ per-event aggregate cap. One reading of the consolidated text gives 7.5 per mille of total sums insured in force; another gives 10 per mille. Either way it is an aggregate scheme cap that scales all claims down pro rata in a national event, not a per-property limit. Get a written confirmation from NTÍ before setting the reinsurance and self-insurance layers.
- Verify the VAT input-tax adjustment (leiðrétting) period for real property with RSK. It is commonly cited as 20 years for buildings and 5 for other assets, but the pro-rata formula and the deregistration triggers matter more than the headline: a sale, a change of use, or letting to a VAT-exempt occupier inside the period all claw back a slice of roughly EUR 35m of recovered construction VAT.
- Confirm Mosfellsbær's actual C-class fasteignaskattur rate and surcharge in its published álagningarhlutföll. The statutory ceiling is 1.32% plus a surcharge of up to 25% of that rate (1.65%), but municipalities set their own within it, and the number is voted annually — it can move against you after you have built.
- Húsaleigulög nr. 36/1994 art. 2(2) makes the commercial-premises rules derogable, which is a gift and a trap. Anything you do not expressly contract out of applies by default, including the art. 56 notice ladder of 6 / 9 / 12 months and the art. 63 restoration standard. Do it clause by clause; a general 'this Act shall not apply' recital is not reliable.
- The residential provisions of the same Act are NOT derogable. Do not let the crew-housing leases be drafted from the commercial template.
- Iceland has no empty-property rates relief. An unlet lot pays full fasteignaskattur on its assessed value. Budget the empty-lot carrying cost explicitly (estimate EUR 4-6m a year here) and hold it in reserve.
- Confirm the Iceland-US tax treaty permanent-establishment threshold before a US streamer signs. A leased lot plus seconded personnel can create an Icelandic PE; their tax team will raise it late and it can kill a deal at signature.
- The grid allocation is the least-documented and most valuable thing on the balance sheet. Confirm in writing with Veitur and Landsnet whether contracted capacity survives a change of control of the propco, an enforcement sale, and a period of non-consumption. If it does not, a refinancing or a mothball can destroy the asset's alternative-use value silently.
- Brunabótamat is a statutory model valuation, not a reinstatement cost estimate for a 30 m stage with a heavy grid and a 2,400 m2 tank. Assume a material gap and buy top-up cover including debris removal, professional fees, code upgrade and a 36-month delay period. Request an HMS revaluation at practical completion while the cost record is fresh.
- The 35% rebate tier's conditions are cumulative and specific — ISK 350m Icelandic spend, 30+ working days including 10+ shooting days, 50+ crew for 50+ days. A production that misses one drops to 25%, which changes its ability to pay your rent by several percent of budget. Screen it at booking, not at wrap.
- Related-party rent between a family propco and a family production company is the single most audit-exposed number in this structure. It sits at the intersection of Icelandic transfer pricing (documentation mandatory above ISK 1bn revenue, 45-day production deadline) and the rebate's eligible-cost test. Benchmark it against a published external rate card and let at least one genuine third-party production a year to evidence it.
- Act nr. 41/2015 on investment incentives is repealed. Any assumption in a business plan that Iceland offers a standing package of property-tax discounts and reduced corporate rates is out of date; only pre-July-2020 agreements survive, and anything new is a bespoke, ESA-notifiable arrangement with a 12-18 month lead time.
- Build-cost, rent, occupancy and fee-range figures in this specification that are marked estimate are exactly that — reasoned market reckonings, not published facts. The development yield-gap conclusion (roughly 3-4.5% yield on cost against a 6.5-8% required yield) follows from those estimates and should be re-run against real tenders and a real letting before any financing decision.
- Web search quota was exhausted early in this session; every fact above was verified by direct fetch of primary sources (Althingi consolidated statutes, Skatturinn, PwC Worldwide Tax Summaries, filminiceland.com) or of dated news headlines with named publishers. Items I could not reach a primary source for are flagged as estimates in the text or listed here.
